#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:452` — a separated strength unit does not participate in contradiction confidence
- Classification: Same-family
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: `contradiction` is error-severity and blocks write, copy, and Complete, while the low-confidence variant is review-only. For a separated dose, the unit word is the evidence that turns a bare number into dose structure and selects the unit-scoped anchor; excluding its confidence lets uncertain transcript evidence create a false hard block.
- Current behaviour: lines 448–450 resolve the separated unit from the token after the numeric candidate, but line 452 calls `_claim_evidence(segment_index, (entry, candidate))` with only medication and number. With high-confidence `paracetamol 250` and low-confidence `mg`, the parser therefore reports an error-level 250-vs-500 contradiction and source coordinates ending at the number. Attached units do not have this asymmetry because number and unit occupy the same candidate token.
- Desired behaviour: every transcript token required to establish the structured dose claim — medication, quantity, and a separated unit when present — must contribute to `min_probability` and the warning's evidence coordinates. A low-confidence separated unit must grade the mismatch `contradiction_low_confidence`; the same text with all three tokens high-confidence must remain `contradiction`.
- Pattern to follow: build the `involved` tuple at the same branch that proves the dose shape, adding the resolved following-unit token only for separated units; keep attached units represented by their existing single candidate token.
- Pattern siblings: `_claims_from_tokens`' attached-unit branch; `test_note_check.py`'s high-probability-number and low-confidence contradiction tests; `test_note_matrix.py:1464` currently lowers the number rather than the separated unit.
- Invariant: every source token whose identity is necessary to construct a transcript-side claim key or value participates in confidence grading and evidence coordinates.
- Verification: add a red/green separated-unit case with medication and number above threshold and only the unit below it; assert review severity and coordinates spanning through the unit. Retain attached-unit, all-high-confidence separated-unit, and high-probability-`uncertain` number pins.
- Regression risk: warning coordinates for separated-unit contradictions widen by one word, and any caller/rendering test that asserted the old truncated interval must move with the semantic fix; attached-unit behaviour must not change.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — Task 5.2 explicitly owns raw-probability grading of transcript-side contradiction evidence.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Verified by trace (involved tuple was `(entry, candidate)` — the separated unit token resolved the anchor but contributed neither probability nor coordinates), then RED demonstrated: `test_a_low_confidence_separated_unit_grades_to_review` (med+number 0.9, `mg` 0.2, identical statement contexts) produced a hard `contradiction` pre-fix. Fix per the finding's Pattern-to-follow, folded into the round's parser restructure: `_dose_claims` builds `involved = (medication, *atom_tokens)` at the same point that proves the dose shape — for separated atoms that includes the unit token, for attached atoms the single candidate token as before — so `min_probability` and the evidence coordinates now span every token the claim's identity rests on (the invariant, verbatim). Green: the low-confidence-unit case grades `contradiction_low_confidence` review with coords `(0,0,2)` spanning through the unit; the all-high-confidence same-state twin stays `contradiction` error; the attached-unit and high-probability-`uncertain`-number pins retained (the latter refit to an identical-context fixture per PR-MED-002's severity contract, uncertain-flag assertion preserved). Verified red → green; suite green at leg close (1117).
- /fix date: 2026-08-11
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-MED-002: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:584` — dose keys conflate medication history, inventory strength, and an intentional dose change
- Classification: Pre-existing in Phase-5 work
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: a matched medication+unit anchor does not establish that two quantities describe the same clinical state. Dose reduction or titration is ordinary clinical documentation, yet the emitted error is not acknowledgeable: the clinician must retract or regenerate, which can force a legitimate medication change out of the note. The same gap turns a product-strength inventory statement into a false blocker.
- Current behaviour: `_StructuredClaim` carries only type, anchor and value; a dose anchor is exactly `<medication> <canonical-unit>` at lines 454–458. The loops at lines 584–594 compare every authored claim with every quoted claim without section, temporal status, action, or intent. Consequently quoted `I take paracetamol 500 mg each morning` plus authored `Reduce paracetamol to 250 mg twice daily.` emits blocking `contradiction`, although both statements are simultaneously true. Likewise `I have paracetamol 500 mg tablets remaining` conflicts with authored 250 mg: removing count words from the unit vocabulary excludes `2 tablets` as the quantity, but does not exclude a strength followed by count/status wording.
- Desired behaviour: an error-level dose contradiction must require mechanically established identity of the medication fact, including its clinical state/time or equivalent structured context. When the checker cannot prove that identity, a differing strength must fail toward review or silence rather than a hard contradiction.
- Pattern to follow: extend the structured claim key with an explicit, conservatively derived context/status dimension or narrow error-level comparison to a mechanically unambiguous same-state shape; do not add a growing temporal/status deny-list, whose unlisted wording would again fail toward blocking.
- Pattern siblings: authored-vs-authored comparisons have the same problem for staged plan assertions; the stock-strength probe and ordinary current-dose-to-new-dose phrasing should be fixed by the same semantic boundary, not special-cased separately.
- Invariant: `contradiction` may block only two logically incompatible claims about the same medication, unit, and clinical state — sharing medication and unit alone is insufficient.
- Verification: pin current-dose history plus an explicit reduction/titration as non-error; pin product-strength inventory/status wording as non-error; add an authored-authored staged-change case; retain a genuinely same-state 250-vs-500 control that still fires at the intended severity.
- Regression risk: a conservative context requirement reduces automatic dose-conflict coverage. Preserve a narrow, explicitly declared error population and route ambiguous cases toward review/silence, consistent with the checker's documented safer direction.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — Task 5.2 and this invocation explicitly require false-blocking legitimate clinical phrasing to be tested.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Verified by trace (claims keyed on `<medication> <unit>` compared with no state dimension), then RED demonstrated on all three false-blockers: current-dose history vs explicit reduction, product-strength inventory vs authored dose, and an authored-authored staged/split regimen each emitted blocking `contradiction` pre-fix. Fix per the finding's Pattern-to-follow (extend the claim with a conservatively derived context, no deny-list): `_StructuredClaim` gained a dose-only `context` — the assertion's full token sequence with the strength atom collapsed to one placeholder (attached and separated spellings collapse identically) — and `_same_state` requires token-IDENTICAL contexts before the `error` grade: the mechanically provable "same medication fact with incompatible values". Every other matched-anchor difference grades the NEW registered review code `dose_mismatch` (`note.NOTE_WARNING_SEVERITY` extended, nothing re-graded): surfaced beside the assertion, acknowledgeable, never blocks — dose changes, titrations, inventory strengths and history phrasing are ordinary documentation. The narrow error population is explicitly declared and pinned by the retained control (`test_a_same_state_dose_conflict_still_blocks`: "Paracetamol 500 mg advised." vs "Paracetamol 250 mg advised." still hard-blocks; the quoted/authored identical-context twin ditto). Green: reduce/inventory/staged cases all `dose_mismatch` review; the three sibling tests whose fixtures were cross-context (window-binding, residue compound in both suites) refit to expect review with docstrings recording why; laterality/negation error grading deliberately untouched (their staged-change analog is not a recorded finding — named residue, not smoothed). No must-pause was needed: the error-level safety control is preserved for the provable population and nothing is silenced — ambiguity lands at review. Verified red → green; suite 1117.
- /fix date: 2026-08-11
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-MED-003: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:437` — common explicit-strength word orders remain silent outside the medication-first window
- Classification: Same-family
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: round 22's dangerous-direction fix claims that an attached or separated explicit strength participates in dose comparison, but the implementation recognises only a medication token followed by a quantity within three tokens. Common, fully structured statements can therefore disagree on a dose and pass with no warning.
- Current behaviour: the dose loop starts only when the current token is a medication and scans the next `_DOSE_WINDOW` tokens. It cannot represent an explicit strength before its medication (`250 mg of paracetamol`) and it stops before an explicit strength in `paracetamol at a dose of 250 mg`. Both forms carry medication, numeric quantity, and canonical strength unit, yet direct probes against authored 500 mg returned no contradiction. The `_DOSE_WINDOW` comment calls its listed residue complete but names neither inverse order nor explicit strengths beyond the three-token forward grammar, and no silence or positive test pins either boundary.
- Desired behaviour: recognise conservative, mechanically explicit strength structures in the ordinary inverse and dose-phrase word orders, or — if the practitioner deliberately accepts their silence — name these structural forms in the bounded residue and pin them. Given that both examples contain the full medication+quantity+strength relation without conversion or semantic guessing, the preferred fix is to compare them.
- Pattern to follow: parse the local numeric-strength atom first (attached or separated), then bind it to a medication through a finite allow-list of explicit grammatical relations such as `<medication> ... <quantity><unit>` and `<quantity><unit> of <medication>`; do not widen the positional window without retaining strength and relation structure.
- Pattern siblings: `_dose_quantity`, the attached/separated positive tests in `test_note_check.py`, the first-quantity-decides rule, and the matrix dose fixtures. Keep temporal numbers, measurements, counts, cross-unit pairs, and unit-less quantities silent.
- Invariant: a common word-order variation must not erase an otherwise explicit same-unit medication-strength discrepancy, and new grammar must not re-admit proximity-only numbers.
- Verification: add red/green contradictions for `250 mg of paracetamol` and `paracetamol at a dose of 250 mg` against authored 500 mg, plus structurally similar temporal/count/measurement negatives; rerun all residue pins and the Property-2 matched-population cells.
- Regression risk: broader relation matching can recreate rounds 19–22's false positives if it becomes a window search. Keep the accepted grammar closed and exact, and prove every new branch with both a positive and its nearest non-dose negative.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — this is the same attached/separated explicit-strength class round 22 intended to close, in the dangerous false-negative direction.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Verified by trace (the loop started only at medication tokens scanning forward, so `250 mg of paracetamol` and `paracetamol at a dose of 250 mg` never produced a claim), then RED demonstrated: both probes emitted NOTHING pre-fix. Fix per the finding's Pattern-to-follow — atom-first, closed relations, never a widened window: `_strength_atoms` finds explicit number+unit atoms (attached or separated) anywhere in the token stream, and `_bound_medication` binds each through EXACTLY three relations — R1 medication-first (the rounds-19-22 grammar with first-quantity-decides digit hygiene preserved), R2 `<atom> of <medication>` exact adjacency, R3 the literal phrase `dose of` immediately before the atom with the medication within the bounded non-digit gap. An atom bound through no relation is silent, which makes the residue COMPLETE-BY-CONSTRUCTION (silent forms = quantities that never become a bound atom), rewritten in the `_DOSE_WINDOW` comment and module docstring per the recurring claim-outruns-structure class. Green: both word orders now surface — as `dose_mismatch` review, the honest severity under PR-MED-002's same-state contract since their statement contexts differ from the authored wording (surfaced, never silent; identical-context conflicts still error) — and every new branch carries its nearest non-dose negative (`2 of the paracetamol tablets`, `20 minutes of the track`, both pinned silent). All rounds-19-22 silence pins, the Property-2 matched-population cells, and both matrix dose cells retained green. Verified red → green; suites at leg close: ruff clean, mypy strict 28 files, pytest **1117 passed**.
- /fix date: 2026-08-11
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:483` — medication-first binding shadows the exact inverse relation and can bind an explicit strength to the wrong entity
- Classification: Fix-induced
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: the round-23 false-negative fix is not closed under ordinary multi-medication wording. A preceding medication inside R1's broad window steals an atom that has the exact R2 shape `<atom> of <medication>`, so a real paracetamol discrepancy can again be silent; the same broad R1 also treats a nearby explicit volume such as water as the preceding medication's dose and can manufacture a false blocker.
- Current behaviour: `_bound_medication` evaluates R1 first and returns immediately on the first preceding lexicon medication with no intervening digit. R2 is considered only afterward. The probe `ibuprofen with 250 mg of paracetamol advised` therefore yields `ibuprofen mg=250`, not `paracetamol mg=250`; an authored paracetamol 500 mg claim has no matched anchor and produces no warning. Separately, `paracetamol mixed with 5 ml water` satisfies R1 and yields `paracetamol ml=5`, despite the atom describing the mixing volume rather than medication strength.
- Desired behaviour: binding must be unambiguous and relation-specific. An exact local relation (R2 `<atom> of <medication>` and the literal R3 `dose of`) must not be shadowed by an ambient R1 candidate; ambiguous competing candidates must fail toward silence/review, and R1 must not claim an explicit measurement merely because it lies within the non-digit window.
- Pattern to follow: resolve relation candidates before returning — prefer the exact grammar that contains both atom and medication, or reject competing bindings — then narrow R1 to a finite medication-first grammar whose connectors are part of the relation rather than treating every non-digit gap as equivalent.
- Pattern siblings: `desktop/tests/test_note_check.py:542` (inverse positive lacks a preceding medication), `:375` (multi-medication clean case uses medication-first order only), and `:392` (measurement clean case contains no medication). These three fixtures do not exercise the cross-product that exposes the mis-binding.
- Invariant: one strength atom may bind to at most one mechanically unambiguous medication, and a more explicit relation must never lose to a weaker positional candidate.
- Verification: add a red/green multi-medication inverse case (`ibuprofen with 250 mg of paracetamol` versus authored paracetamol 500 mg) that must surface on the paracetamol anchor, plus a medication-adjacent non-dose strength-unit measurement (`paracetamol mixed with 5 ml water`) that must not hard-block. Retain all three existing positive relations and the rounds-19–23 silence pins.
- Regression risk: changing precedence can move anchors in multi-medication statements; narrowing R1 can silence valid free-word-order doses. Keep the three accepted relations explicit and add paired positive/negative fixtures for every permitted connector.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — Task 5.2 and this confirmation round explicitly require tracing all three relations for mis-binding and preventing recurrence of the rounds-19–22 false-positive/false-negative classes.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Verified by trace (R1 returned before R2 was consulted; R1's gap admitted ANY non-digit word), then RED demonstrated: `ibuprofen with 250 mg of paracetamol` surfaced NOTHING against authored paracetamol 500 mg (the atom bound to ibuprofen), and `paracetamol mixed with 5 ml water` emitted a dose warning against an authored volume. Fix per the finding's Pattern-to-follow, a NARROWING not a broadening: `_dose_binding` COLLECTS candidates across all three relations before deciding — exact-inverse R2 first, dose-phrase R3, then medication-first R1 — and two relations naming DIFFERENT medications are ambiguous and bind NOTHING (silence, never a guessed anchor: the invariant verbatim). R1 and R3's gaps are no longer "any non-digit tokens": `_connector_walk` accepts only the closed `_DOSE_CONNECTORS` set (`at`, `to`, `a`, `the`), so `mixed`/`with` break the relation and the mixing volume is silent; `_digit_bearing_between` became dead code and was REMOVED (first-quantity-decides is now implied by connector closure — digit-bearing gap tokens are not connectors). Green: the multi-medication inverse surfaces on the PARACETAMOL anchor; the mixing volume is silent; every rounds-19–23 positive relation, silence pin, clean cell and matrix fixture retained green (the multi-med compound and `Reduce … to 250 mg` traces re-verified under the narrowed connectors). Residue updated complete-by-construction: non-connector gaps and ambiguous bindings are named silent forms. Verified red → green; final suite 1123.
- /fix date: 2026-08-11
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-MED-002: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:524` — low-confidence `of` / `dose of` relation words can still create an error-grade dose contradiction
- Classification: Fix-induced
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: R2 and R3 exist only because literal relation words bind the atom to a medication. If one of those words is acoustically uncertain, the checker has uncertain evidence for the claim identity; ignoring that uncertainty can turn a dubious parse into an unacknowledgeable hard block, the same safety/severity defect round 23 fixed for a separated unit.
- Current behaviour: `_dose_claims` passes only `(medication, *atom_tokens)` to `_claim_evidence`. R2's required `of` and R3's required `dose of` tokens are absent. In the inverse probe with medication/number/unit at 0.9 and only `of` at 0.2, the claim retained `min_probability=0.9`; an otherwise identical 250-vs-500 pair therefore routes through `_same_state` to error-grade `contradiction`, not `contradiction_low_confidence`. The coordinate interval happens to span the relation word because it lies between atom and medication, but its probability is not considered.
- Desired behaviour: every transcript token whose literal identity is required to select a dose-binding relation must participate in confidence grading. At minimum R2 must include `of`, and R3 must include both `dose` and `of`; the implementation should make the involved-token set a result of the chosen relation so future relation additions cannot omit their evidence.
- Pattern to follow: have `_bound_medication` return a binding object (medication index plus relation kind/evidence indices) rather than only an index, and derive `_claim_evidence` from that complete binding plus the atom.
- Pattern siblings: round-23 PR-MED-001's separated-unit invariant; `desktop/tests/test_note_check.py:663` covers a low-confidence unit but no relation token; `:542` and `:560` cover R2/R3 only at default high confidence.
- Invariant: every source token necessary to establish a transcript-side claim's anchor, value, or binding relation contributes to `min_probability` and evidence coordinates.
- Verification: add same-context R2 and R3 conflicts with only `of`, then only `dose`, below `UNCERTAINTY_THRESHOLD`; each must grade `contradiction_low_confidence` review and point across the complete relation. Retain high-confidence twins as error and the separated-unit regression.
- Regression risk: widening the evidence set can demote a real conflict when a connector is low-confidence. That is the intended conservative direction; medication/atom confidence and all-high-confidence error controls must remain pinned.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — this is the direct new-relation sibling of round-23 PR-MED-001 and Task 5.2's raw-probability severity contract.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Verified by trace (`_dose_claims` passed only medication+atom to `_claim_evidence`), then RED demonstrated: same-context R2 and R3 conflicts with only `of` (resp. only `dose`) at 0.2 both produced hard `contradiction` instead of the review grade. Fix exactly as the finding's Pattern-to-follow prescribes: `_bound_medication` became `_dose_binding` returning a BINDING OBJECT — medication index plus the indices of every relation token whose literal identity established the binding (connectors, `of`, `dose`) — and `_dose_claims` derives `_claim_evidence` from the COMPLETE binding (medication + relation evidence + atom tokens), so the involved-token set is a RESULT of the chosen relation and a future relation cannot omit its evidence (the finding's future-proofing clause, satisfied structurally). Green: low-confidence `of` grades `contradiction_low_confidence` with coordinates spanning the full relation (0,0,3); low-confidence `dose` likewise; the high-confidence R2/R3 twins stay error; the round-23 separated-unit regression and the all-high-confidence controls retained. The intended conservative direction is documented: a low-confidence connector now demotes a real conflict to review — acknowledged in the finding's own Regression-risk line as intended. Verified red → green; final suite 1123.
- /fix date: 2026-08-11
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-MED-003: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:692` — token-identical context is not sufficient to make inventory strengths mutually exclusive
- Classification: Fix-induced
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: the stated severity contract says inventory strengths never hard-block, yet a patient can legitimately have tablets of two strengths at home. Treating two otherwise identical inventory statements as logically incompatible creates an error that blocks write, copy, and Complete and can force legitimate medication information out of the note.
- Current behaviour: `_same_state` equates any two dose contexts that become token-identical after the atom is collapsed. The probes `I have paracetamol 500 mg tablets remaining` and `I have paracetamol 250 mg tablets remaining` both collapse to `i have paracetamol <dose> tablets remaining`, so `_same_state` returns true and the comparison loops emit blocking `contradiction`. The existing inventory regression compares inventory wording with `Paracetamol 250 mg advised.`; it proves only the different-context path to `dose_mismatch`, not that inventory strengths themselves never block.
- Desired behaviour: error-grade identity must prove an exclusive medication-dose slot, not merely textual sameness after removing the value. Inventory/product-strength membership and other multi-valued contexts must grade `dose_mismatch` review even when their surrounding tokens are identical; semantically equivalent but mechanically unproved current-dose paraphrases may remain review by the declared conservative boundary.
- Pattern to follow: define a closed positive grammar for the narrow error population (an explicitly current administration/regimen fact with one exclusive strength slot), or carry a structured relation/context class whose inventory/product-strength form is never error-grade. Do not repair this with an open-ended status deny-list.
- Pattern siblings: `desktop/tests/test_note_check.py:612` (inventory versus advised dose only), `:630` (staged wording differs), and `:646` (the retained hard-error control uses `advised` but does not prove that the surrounding context is an exclusive state).
- Invariant: `contradiction` may block only claims that cannot both be true; token identity after value collapse is necessary but not sufficient for multi-valued inventory/product facts.
- Verification: add quoted/authored and authored/authored pairs for two simultaneously held paracetamol tablet strengths with otherwise identical wording; both must emit `dose_mismatch`, never `contradiction`. Replace or supplement the hard-error control with a fixture whose closed grammar actually establishes one current exclusive dose slot, and retain reduction/staged-regimen review cases.
- Regression risk: narrowing the error population can downgrade genuine dose conflicts. Keep every differing matched anchor surfaced as `dose_mismatch` and pin the exact positive grammar chosen for the remaining hard-error population.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — this is the round-23 severity contract's explicit inventory-strength promise and the invocation specifically asks whether legitimate inventory can still hard-block.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Verified by trace (`_same_state` was bare context token-identity, so twin inventory statements hard-blocked), then RED demonstrated: `I have paracetamol 500 mg tablets remaining` vs the 250 mg twin emitted blocking `contradiction` in both quoted/authored and authored/authored pairings. Fix per the finding's Pattern-to-follow — a CLOSED POSITIVE grammar for the error population, no status deny-list: `_same_state` now requires identical contexts AND `_exclusive_context` — every context token drawn from the closed `_EXCLUSIVE_DOSE_CONTEXT` allow-list (administration verbs, frequency/timing words, dose-relation words) plus medication names — i.e. the wording of an explicitly current regimen fact with ONE strength slot, the only shape whose differing values cannot both be true. Inventory wording (`have`/`tablets`/`remaining`) and ANY novel/unlisted wording fall outside the grammar and grade `dose_mismatch` review even when token-identical — the allow-list direction, so unlisted wording fails toward review, never toward blocking (the invariant: token identity is necessary but not sufficient). Green: both inventory pairings grade review; the hard-error population is pinned POSITIVELY (`test_the_exclusive_grammar_error_population_is_pinned`: `I take paracetamol N mg twice daily`, plus the retained `advised` and timing-phrase controls, all re-verified inside the grammar); reduction/staged-regimen review cases retained. Named residue, per the finding's own allowance: semantically-current paraphrases OUTSIDE the closed grammar stay review — the declared conservative boundary. Verification note, recorded honestly: the first green run's ONE failure was `test_recorder_no_sockets_during_real_whisper_transcription` — an untouched live-ML integration test whose child hit its 300 s transcription deadline during a machine-load-degraded 40 min suite run (all 1122 others green, every note suite green); a clean rerun passed 1123/1123 in 64 s, confirming load-flake, not a regression — no note-pipeline code is in that test's path. Final suites: ruff clean, mypy strict 28 files, pytest **1123 passed**.
- /fix date: 2026-08-11
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/session_store.py` (`_canonical_note`) — refusal message embedded pydantic ValidationError detail, which renders clinical input values
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast (mechanical message change; the convention is established)
- Why it matters: a pydantic error's rendered detail includes `input_value=` reprs — i.e. note span text. The clinical-artifact message convention is TERSE messages (`_verified_note`'s "note artifact is malformed", `read_transcript`; `_parse_config_blob`'s docstring records the deliberate contrast for non-clinical config). No live logging path existed (UI labels only; `tasks.py` never logs) and the tripwire's registered loc-path/`span_text` signatures were a real backstop — hence MED, not HIGH — but the convention exists precisely so safety does not depend on the backstop. The rounds-3–24 message/claim-discipline family at a new site.
- Current behaviour: `NoteWriteRefusedError(f"note artifact failed canonical re-validation: {exc}")`.
- Desired behaviour: terse message, exception chained (`from exc`) for debugging; the string safe to display or log anywhere.
- Pattern to follow: `_verified_note` / `read_transcript` terse messages.
- Pattern siblings: `finalise_note`/`NoteDraft` construction can propagate raw pydantic ValidationErrors — consistent with note.py's Phase-1-accepted posture (constructors raise raw validation errors; the terse-wrapping convention applies at the ARTIFACT-I/O boundary, which `_canonical_note` is) — no change there.
- Verification: existing `test_refuses_forged_validator_skipping_note` matches on "canonical re-validation", unchanged by the terser message.
- Regression risk: none — message-only; the exception type and chaining are unchanged.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Message made terse with an in-code comment recording the convention and why (`input values = note text; the string must stay safe to display or log anywhere`). Chain retained. Test regex unaffected.

- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/session.py:519,537-553` — `discard()` can delete key custody after a generation lease is acquired
- Classification: 🆕 Pre-existing
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: `SessionController` promises any-thread-safe controls (`session.py:245-248`), and `GenerationInProgressError` says a custody-destructive operation is refused while the lease is held (`:207-210`). That invariant is false for a precise interleaving: thread A passes `_refuse_while_generating("discard")` at `:519`, drops `_lock`, and blocks in `worker.stop()` at `:538`; thread B then acquires the state-agnostic lease at `begin_generation()` (`:584-597`); thread A reacquires `_lock` at `:539` and reaches key-first `discard_session()` at `:553` without another guard. Key deletion/crypto destruction can therefore run while `controller.generating` is true. The current intended same-session generation states normally avoid a live capture worker, so this is MED rather than HIGH, but the lease is deliberately global and target-agnostic and the irreversible custody contract cannot depend on that caller convention.
- Current behaviour: lease exclusion is checked only before the unlocked worker-stop interval; no reservation prevents acquisition during the interval and no post-stop guard exists.
- Desired behaviour: make discard and generation acquisition mutually exclusive across the whole two-lock discard operation. Reserve the custody transition under `_lock` before stopping the worker and make `begin_generation()` refuse/serialize against that reservation; clear it on every exit. A bare post-stop recheck is insufficient because refusal at that point leaves the session claiming RECORDING/PAUSED after its worker has already been stopped.
- Pattern to follow: the controller's existing guarded state flags (`_generation`, `_LiveSession.transcribing`) establish ownership under `_lock` before work crosses a thread boundary. Use the same reservation shape for this destructive transition while preserving the established rule that the worker join stays outside `_lock`.
- Pattern siblings: no other Phase 6 custody-destructive path has this gap. `complete()`, `complete_recovered()`, `discard_recovered()`, `destroy_recovered_crypto()`, and `_retire_locked()` keep guard and mutation in one locked critical section; `finish()` releases the lock around `worker.stop()` but does not delete a key or retire crypto.
- Invariant: after `begin_generation()` returns, no start/Complete/Discard/retirement/recovered-key-destruction mutation may begin until that exact lease ends; conversely, generation acquisition must not enter after a custody-destructive operation has reserved its transition.
- Verification: add a deterministic two-thread barrier test using the existing `TestDiscardStartRace` worker-wrapper pattern: pause `discard()` inside `worker.stop()`, attempt `begin_generation()`, and prove either the lease acquisition is refused/serialized until discard completes or discard is refused before any key/crypto mutation. Assert there is never a state in which the lease is held while `discard_session()` deletes the key, and retain the existing worker-stop-outside-lock/deadlock regression.
- Regression risk: holding `_lock` across `worker.stop()` can deadlock with capture callbacks, while merely rechecking after stop can strand a half-stopped live session. The repair needs explicit transition ownership plus `finally` cleanup, and must preserve concurrent-discard idempotence and the wrong-session protection pinned by `TestDiscardStartRace`.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — Task 6.3 is specifically the cross-thread custody coordinator, and the phase's Done condition says the lease blocks Discard for its full lifetime.

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py:1668,1753,1869` — provider output can bypass the proposal/clinician-confirmation path
- Classification: Pre-existing
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: Flow 1 says the provider contributes transcript assertions and every `autofill`/`prefill` assertion must originate as a proposal whose exact text the clinician resolved. That invariant is not enforced at the provider boundary. A valid `NoteModelProvider` can return a `GeneratedSection` containing a `prefill` `NoteAssertion` with a self-constructed matching `ConfirmationDecision`, shown-text digest, and config digest. `NoteDraft` accepts it; `finalise_note` carries provider sections through without correlating them to `note_proposals`/`resolutions`; Check 3 emits only the non-blocking `clinician_asserted` review for prefill provenance (`note_check.py:906-919`); and `write_note` verifies the self-consistent fields but has no emitted-proposal membership to check (`session_store.py:786-821`). The assertion can therefore reach `note.enc` without ever being displayed or confirmed. The current shipping `ExtractiveNoteProvider` does not exercise the gap, so this is MED rather than HIGH, but the provider Protocol is the deliberate Phase-3B extension seam and the artifact-level confirmation claim must not depend on every provider behaving.
- Current behaviour: `compose_draft()` accepts `provider.generate_sections(request)` wholesale (`note.py:1750-1766`). `NoteDraft._check_draft()` validates section order and duplicate assertion IDs only (`:1668-1692`), and `finalise_note()` validates resolutions only against `draft.note_proposals` before merging the untouched base sections (`:1826-1869`). No stage establishes that every non-transcript assertion came from one emitted proposal and one supplied confirmation resolution.
- Desired behaviour: confine the provider/base-note side of the draft to transcript-provenance assertions, and re-establish that stage-specific invariant at finalisation so a forged/validator-skipping draft cannot bypass it. Every final non-transcript assertion must be introduced only by the proposal-resolution loop. If Phase 3B later adds model-authored provenance, give it a distinct explicit contract rather than widening the `autofill`/`prefill` path.
- Pattern to follow: the existing `NoteProposal`/`NoteAssertion` type split and the config/request use-time boundary — enforce the semantic route at the consuming boundary, not by trusting an implementation convention or enumerating the two current providers.
- Pattern siblings: `ExtractiveNoteProvider` and every current `MockNoteModelProvider` behaviour emit transcript-provenance base assertions and should remain valid; the future Phase-3B provider is the important sibling because the plan promises it can use this seam without bypassing confirmation.
- Invariant: every `autofill`/`prefill` assertion in a final note corresponds one-to-one to a `NoteProposal` emitted in that draft and a confirmed `ProposalResolution` whose displayed-text digest matches; provider base sections contain transcript assertions only.
- Verification: add a stub provider returning a fully schema-valid `prefill` assertion with fabricated-but-internally-consistent confirmation evidence and no proposal; prove compose/finalise refuses it and no `note.enc` can be written. Retain the legitimate Extractive/Mock pipeline tests and the confirmed/declined/pending proposal matrix; add a validator-skipping `NoteDraft` case if finalisation claims to re-establish the invariant.
- Regression risk: a stage-specific restriction can accidentally reject the existing adversarial Mock provider or pre-empt Phase 3B's future provenance design. Keep `GeneratedSection` broad for final saved notes; narrow only provider/base-draft ingestion and finalisation, with the future model provenance left to its own explicit phase contract.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — Task 6.1 owns the two-stage pipeline and explicitly claims unconfirmed content cannot cross it; this is the direct enforcement gap in that claim.

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/session.py:507-523,525-587` — an in-flight Discard does not exclude Complete, so both terminal actions can mutate the same queued session
- Classification: Pre-existing
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: `SessionController` promises that all controls are safe from any thread (`session.py:245-248`), but a queued-session `discard()` reserves and then necessarily releases `_lock` between its two critical sections (`:528-565`). The reservation is consulted only by `begin_generation()` (`:625-636`), not by `complete()` (`:507-523`). In the valid interleaving where Discard passes its first section and thread B enters `complete()` during that unlocked gap, Complete verifies/deletes custody, transitions the shared `live.session` QUEUED → WRITTEN, clears `_live`, and returns success. Discard then reacquires the lock, sees WRITTEN rather than its sole early-return state DISCARDED (`:573-575`), calls key-first `discard_session()` and removes the session directory (`:576-579`), then raises `IllegalTransitionError` trying WRITTEN → DISCARDED (`:580`). One caller has therefore been told the session completed as WRITTEN while the overlapping call removed its artifacts, and the Discard caller raises only after its destructive side effect. Shipped GUI callbacks are single-threaded, so this is MED rather than HIGH, but it violates the controller's explicit any-thread contract at a custody transition.
- Current behaviour: `_custody_reservations` excludes only generation acquisition; `complete()` can enter and complete the exact live snapshot while a queued `discard()` owns the two-lock transition.
- Desired behaviour: once Discard reserves its transition, Complete must refuse or serialize before `complete_session()` performs any custody mutation. Preserve concurrent-Discard idempotence and the existing concurrent-Start snapshot behavior; do not hold `_lock` across `worker.stop()`.
- Pattern to follow: `begin_generation()` already consumes `_custody_reservations` under the controller lock (`session.py:625-636`). Reuse that transition-ownership signal at the conflicting Complete entry rather than adding a post-mutation state repair or another unlocked check.
- Pattern siblings: `complete()` is the only same-live-session terminal operation that can legally pass its state gate while a queued Discard is between lock sections. `start()` deliberately operates on a new snapshot and is covered by `TestDiscardStartRace`; overlapping Discards remain admitted by the counter; recovered Complete/Discard operations keep guard and mutation inside one locked section.
- Invariant: after `discard()` reserves a live session's custody transition, no conflicting Complete may verify/delete custody or return WRITTEN for that session until the reservation is released; exactly one terminal action wins before either reports success.
- Verification: add a deterministic two-thread barrier at the queued Discard's first-lock exit (an instrumented lock or equivalent rendezvous, never a scheduler sleep), attempt `complete()` while the reservation is held, and prove it refuses/serializes before `complete_session`; then release Discard and assert it alone reaches DISCARDED with key-first deletion. Retain the round-27 generation-window test, concurrent-Discard cleanup, worker-stop-outside-lock, and `TestDiscardStartRace` pins.
- Regression risk: a broad reservation refusal could accidentally reject the intentionally legal second Discard or the concurrent-Start/new-session path; holding `_lock` across `worker.stop()` would reintroduce the capture-callback deadlock. Keep the new exclusion scoped to conflicting Complete semantics and under the existing lock.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — Task 6.3 owns the any-thread custody coordinator and the defect is in the reservation-protected terminal transition added/hardened in this phase.

#### LEG 1 verified tuples (executor `stage-6`, 2026-08-11 — verification only, no code changed)

One honest nuance recorded, which SUPPORTS the MED grade: by the time the rmtree runs, Complete has already legitimately destroyed the key after full verification, so every removed artifact is already cryptographically dead — the harm is the contract violation itself (two terminal actions both mutating one session, the destructive act preceding the failure report, and inconsistent caller outcomes), not a wrongful key deletion. - **CLASS BOUNDEDNESS — verdict: BOUNDED. One point-fix (`complete()` consults `_custody_reservations` under its existing single locked section) closes the class.** The enumeration behind the verdict, checked entry point by entry point against the current tree: `discard()` is the ONLY custody-mutating operation with an unlocked window — `complete()`, `complete_recovered()`, `discard_recovered()`, `destroy_recovered_crypto()`, `start()` (including `_retire_locked`), and `end_generation()` each hold `_lock` for their entire body, and the operations that DO release the lock mid-flight (`finish()`, `pause()`, `transcribe()`) mutate no custody (store sealing/closing is not key/crypto destruction, and their races are covered by the PR-HIGH-001 snapshot pins and the `transcribing` flag). What can slot into discard's window, case by case: `begin_generation` — guarded (round 27); `complete` — THIS finding, the one missing consumer; a SECOND `discard` — deliberately admitted and safe (the counter admits it, the DISCARDED early-return plus `discard_session`'s idempotence handle either completion order); `start` — deliberately admitted and pinned safe by `TestDiscardStartRace` (retirement destroys the in-memory key copy only, on-disk custody untouched, and discard's snapshot discipline protects the new session); `mark_queued`/`transcribe`/`finish`/`pause`/`resume` — state-excluded (a QUEUED discardee fails their state gates; conversely for a RECORDING/PAUSED/FAILED discardee it is `complete()` that is state-refused, so the Complete conflict is exactly and only the QUEUED case the peer traced); the recovered coordinator ops — single locked sections over RECOVERED directories, disjoint from the live session by reachability (the recovery listing excludes the controller's non-terminal session and checkout discipline blocks list actions) — passing the LIVE session's directory/crypto to a recovered op would be parameter misuse of a differently-scoped API, a contract boundary worth naming, not a concurrency hole; the 24 h sweep — externally protected (rounds 42/18 wiring, re-verified in round 25). Cross-pairs: complete-vs-complete serializes on the single section (the loser fails `_require_live`); generation-vs-complete is guarded at complete's entry today. CONCLUSION: after the one fix, every conflicting custody-mutating entry point is guarded, deliberately-admitted-and-pinned, state-excluded, scope-disjoint, or externally protected — a holistic custody-operation serialization layer is NOT needed and would re-litigate the load-bearing worker-join-outside-lock design for no additional coverage.
- Fix-shape note for the fix leg: mirror `begin_generation`'s consumer shape — check `_custody_reservations` inside `complete()`'s existing locked section at entry (beside the lease check, before `_require_state`), refusing with a typed activity error naming the in-flight discard; the reservation must keep refusing NOTHING else (the second-discard and start admissions are pinned), and no lock may be held across any worker join (unchanged — complete has no join).
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Applied exactly per the leg-1 fix-shape note and the peer's Pattern to follow. `complete()` now consults `_custody_reservations` inside its EXISTING single locked section at entry — after the lease check, before `_require_state` — refusing with `SessionActivityError("a discard is completing; the session cannot be completed")`; the in-code comment records the invariant (exactly one terminal action wins before either reports success) and the deliberate non-broadening (second discards and concurrent `start()` stay admitted; the reservation's only consumers are `begin_generation` and now `complete`). No lock is held across any worker join (complete has none; discard unchanged). Verification added exactly as prescribed: `test_complete_refused_inside_discard_unlocked_window` is a deterministic TWO-THREAD barrier — a `_BarrierWorker` injected into the QUEUED session rendezvouses via `threading.Event`s (no scheduler sleeps): the discard thread parks INSIDE its unlocked window with the reservation held, the main thread's `complete()` is proven refused BEFORE `complete_session` mutates anything (key file asserted intact at that instant), then the released discard alone reaches DISCARDED with key-first deletion and directory removal, `controller.session` ending None. Retained untouched and still green by construction: the round-27 generation-window barrier test, the reservation `finally`-discipline test, concurrent-discard idempotence, worker-stop-outside-lock, and both `TestDiscardStartRace` pins (the reservation still refuses nothing they exercise). Class closure per the accepted leg-1 enumeration: with this consumer added, every entry point that can slot into discard's window is guarded, admitted-and-pinned, state-excluded, scope-disjoint, or externally protected. Composer verification pending (verify=composer); expected pytest 1175 + 1 = 1176.

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/session.py:331-336,538-600` — concurrent Start can expose a still-reserved Discard target to recovery and the expiry sweep
- Classification: Same-family
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: `discard()` snapshots live session X, increments the custody reservation, and releases `_lock` around `worker.stop()` (`session.py:561-578`). The deliberately admitted `start()` can then retire X and install Y: `_retire_locked()` destroys only X's old in-memory crypto while deliberately leaving X's `key.dpapi` and directory recoverable (`session.py:331-336,740-756`). From that instant, the controller/UI active-session exclusion names Y, not still-reserved X (`ui/main_window.py:126-134`), and `list_recoverable_sessions()` will list any non-excluded X with a live key blob (`ui/models.py:148-218`). A recovery worker can therefore call `unwrap_key_from_file(X)` (`transcription.py:1007`) before Discard resumes, creating a second `SessionCrypto` for X's key. Discard then deletes X's custody blob and destroys only its old snapshot crypto (`session.py:578-600`), while the separately unwrapped recovered key can survive and continue being used. Cryptographic deletion has returned success while an app-owned plaintext key remains in memory. The same missing reserved-ID protection lets the 24 h sweep target X after retirement; recovered Complete/Discard likewise accept a directory without testing whether that session ID is reserved (`session.py:675-688`). The normal GUI is single-threaded, so reaching this scheduling window still requires a non-GUI custody caller/injected race and the finding is MED rather than HIGH, consistent with rounds 27 and 29, but it falsifies the controller's documented any-thread custody claim and the asserted class closure.
- Current behaviour: `_custody_reservations` is a target-agnostic count consumed by live Complete and generation acquisition only. It proves an operation is in flight but cannot keep that operation's session ID in the recovery/sweep protection set after `start()` replaces `_live`, nor can a recovered coordinator call test whether its directory is the reserved target.
- Desired behaviour: retain target-aware custody reservations for every in-flight Discard and treat each reserved session ID as protected from recovery checkout, recovered terminal operations, and expiry sweeping until that Discard exits. Preserve the deliberately admitted Start and second-Discard behavior unless the product intentionally changes that policy.
- Pattern to follow: the existing checkout/active-session identity sets at the recovery and sweep boundaries, but source the additional IDs from controller-owned per-session reservation counts rather than from the mutable current `_live` pointer. Re-check the same reservation by resolved session identity inside recovered coordinator entry points so a stale/direct callback fails closed as well as disappearing from the list.
- Pattern siblings: `MainWindow._live_session_ids()`, `app.sweep_protected_ids()`, `RecoveryScreen` checkout protection, `complete_recovered()`, `discard_recovered()`, and the raw recovery unwrap boundary. A global boolean is insufficient: overlapping Discards need independent per-ID lifetime, and one completion must not unprotect another target.
- Invariant: from the instant Discard reserves session X until its `finally` releases the reservation, no path may unwrap X's on-disk key, complete/discard X through the recovered coordinator, or sweep X; starting session Y must not make X externally recoverable during that interval.
- Verification: add a deterministic barrier that parks Discard(X) after reservation, admits `start()` to install Y, and then proves X is absent from the recovery listing and included in the sweep protection set; direct recovered coordinator/checkout attempts for X must refuse before unwrapping or mutating custody. Release Discard and prove its key-first deletion completes, the reservation clears, and Y's custody is intact. Retain the round-27 generation barrier, round-29 Complete barrier, second-Discard counter/finally coverage, and worker-stop-outside-lock regression. The existing `TestDiscardStartRace` proves only that Discard keeps targeting X rather than Y; it does not prove X remains unavailable to a third custody path during the reservation.
- Regression risk: a global protection bit can retain unrelated expired sessions, and a set without per-ID reference counts can be cleared too early by overlapping Discards. Do not move `worker.stop()` under the controller lock or reintroduce the capture-callback deadlock; keep the repair identity-scoped across the current two-lock shape.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — Task 6.3 declares one lease-aware coordinator for live and recovered custody, and this is the missed composition between its admitted live-session retirement and recovered/sweep boundaries.

#### LEG 1 verification (executor `stage-6`, 2026-08-11, folded into this verify-then-fix leg per the practitioner's disposition)
- PR-MED-001: **real=yes; materiality=behavioral; severity=MED (agrees — same non-GUI-thread reachability grading as rounds 27/29); scope=IN-SCOPE.** Confirmed against the current tree exactly as the peer traced: the reservation was a target-agnostic count consumed only by `begin_generation`/`complete`; `_retire_locked` deliberately leaves X's `key.dpapi` + directory recoverable while destroying only the in-memory copy; the recovery exclusion and sweep protection were sourced from the MUTABLE live pointer, which the admitted `start()` swaps mid-window; and neither recovered-coordinator op tested the reservation by identity. My round-29 BOUNDED enumeration is honestly FALSIFIED on its recovered-path premise — I judged the recovered surface "scope-disjoint by reachability (the listing excludes the controller's non-terminal session)" without noticing that the admitted start() changes that exclusion's REFERENT while the reservation is still held. The composition of two individually-sound admissions was the gap; recorded so round 31 verifies the structural fix rather than re-deriving the class.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Applied the practitioner-approved STRUCTURAL fix exactly as Desired behaviour / Pattern to follow prescribe. (1) `SessionController._custody_reservations` is now a TARGET-AWARE `dict[str, int]` — per-session-id counts with independent lifetime (`_reserve_custody_locked`/`_release_custody_locked`; overlapping discards of one id nest, releasing one never unprotects another target; the global-flag insufficiency the finding names is structurally excluded). `discard()` reserves ITS TARGET's id in its first locked section and releases in the same `finally`; the coarse consumers (`begin_generation`, `complete`) keep their exact refusal semantics (`if self._custody_reservations:` over a dict — messages and round-27/29 pins unchanged). (2) NEW `reserved_session_ids()` on the controller (+ `SessionControllerLike` + `FakeController`) is THE protection source: `MainWindow._live_session_ids` unions it with the non-terminal live session (the exclusion no longer depends on the mutable pointer alone), and `app.sweep_protected_ids` unions it into the sweep exemption (docstrings at both sites record the round-30 lesson). (3) The recovered-coordinator entry points fail closed by RESOLVED identity: `complete_recovered`/`discard_recovered` call the new `_refuse_reserved_target_locked`, which — only while reservations exist — resolves the directory's identity through the imported single `_resolve_session_identity` definition (header authoritative, never a caller claim; a resolution failure propagates typed, fail-closed) and refuses a reserved target with a typed `SessionActivityError`; `destroy_recovered_crypto` refuses COARSELY while any reservation is held, documented in code — it carries no directory to resolve, and coarse is a strict superset of scoped, failing toward safety at the cost of a transient retry (`MainWindow._destroy_recovered_crypto`'s catch widened to `(GenerationInProgressError, SessionActivityError)`, reference retained either way). (4) `RecoveryScreen` re-checks the exclusion set at CLICK time for BOTH Resume and list-Discard (`_selection_blocked`: the rendered list can be stale; refusal happens BEFORE any key unwrap or custody mutation, fail-CLOSED on a provider error — deliberately unlike `refresh()`'s fail-open listing posture, reasoned in code). PRESERVED: concurrent `start()` and second-`discard()` admissions (reservations still refuse neither), `worker.stop()` outside the lock, the round-27/29 barrier semantics. Verification added exactly as prescribed: `test_reserved_discard_target_protected_across_live_pointer_swap` — a deterministic barrier (event rendezvous; the `_BarrierWorker` is reentrant-guarded because `start()`'s `_retire_locked` legally re-stops it under the lock, the `TestDiscardStartRace` idiom) parks Discard(X), admits `start()` installing Y, then proves X ∈ `reserved_session_ids()` while Y is not, all three recovered-coordinator attempts on X refuse BEFORE any unwrap/mutation (key intact, foreign crypto untouched), an UNRESERVED recovered directory still completes during the window (identity-scoped, not coarse over-blocking), and on release the discard alone deletes X key-first with the reservation clearing and Y's custody intact; plus `test_sweep_protected_ids_covers_reserved_discard_targets` (app leg), `test_live_session_ids_include_reserved_discard_targets`, the two stale-selection click-time refusal tests (resume + discard), and the widened-catch retention test (UI leg). Round-27/29 barriers, second-discard `finally` coverage, worker-stop-outside-lock, and `TestDiscardStartRace` all retained untouched. Composer verification pending (verify=composer); expected pytest 1176 + 6 = 1182.

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/app.py:128-131`; `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/main_window.py:141-144` — protected-id snapshots can omit a reserved Discard target
- Classification: Fix-induced
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: both protection helpers read controller state in multiple separately locked calls. For a QUEUED/FAILED live session X, `sweep_protected_ids()` can read `active_session_ids() == ∅` and then `reserved_session_ids() == ∅`; Discard(X) can next reserve X at `session.py:590`, and the deliberately admitted `start()` can retire X and install Y at `session.py:332-378`; the helper's final `controller.session` read then sees Y and returns a set that omits X while X's reservation is still live. `sweep_sessions()` may consequently sweep X during the very lifetime the round-30 fix promises to protect. `MainWindow._live_session_ids()` has the same split read in the opposite two-call form, so a recovery refresh — and the click-time stale-selection check that reuses this provider — can likewise observe neither reserved X nor live X even though the reservation is live at return. With a stale X item (obtainable when a refresh snapshots before `start(X)` and scans the directory after its key is created), the second split snapshot can let the recovery runner reach `unwrap_key_from_file(X)` during Discard(X), recreating the surviving-plaintext-key outcome round 30 was meant to close. Reachability still needs non-GUI-thread controller calls, matching rounds 27/29/30's MED grading rather than HIGH, but it violates the controller's explicit any-thread custody contract and falsifies full-lifetime protection.
- Current behaviour: `reserved_session_ids()` is individually correct and locked, as are `active_session_ids()` and `session`, but neither caller obtains the reservation set and current non-terminal live id in one linearizable controller operation. The comments call the resulting unions THE protection source even though their constituent observations can describe different controller states.
- Desired behaviour: expose one controller-owned atomic snapshot that returns every custody-protected id — all current reservation keys plus the current non-terminal live session id — under a single acquisition of `SessionController._lock`, and use that one snapshot for both the sweep and recovery-list/click provider. Preserve `active_session_ids()` if its narrower Phase-2 API is still useful, but do not compose the round-30 protection invariant from separate public reads.
- Pattern to follow: `reserved_session_ids()` already copies controller-owned state under `_lock`; widen that pattern into a single purpose-named snapshot whose whole union is constructed inside the critical section, then make both external consumers delegate to it rather than reconstructing controller state.
- Pattern siblings: `app.sweep_protected_ids()` and `MainWindow._live_session_ids()` are the two shipping consumers and both carry the same race. `RecoveryScreen._selection_blocked()` reuses the latter provider, so it is covered by the same fix; no third shipping consumer of `reserved_session_ids()` exists.
- Invariant: at every linearization point, the protected-id snapshot contains session X if X is either the controller's current non-terminal session or has a positive custody-reservation count. Therefore a Discard(X) → Start(Y) transition cannot produce a snapshot omitting X before Discard(X)'s `finally` releases its last per-id reservation.
- Verification: add deterministic barriers around the old split-read boundary for BOTH consumers: begin the snapshot while X is QUEUED/FAILED and unreserved, pause after the reservation read, run Discard(X) to reservation plus admitted Start(Y), then resume and prove the returned set still contains X. Pin recovery listing/click refusal and an expiry sweep using that atomic snapshot, plus two simultaneous reservations for different ids where releasing one leaves the other protected. Retain all round-27/29/30 barriers, worker-stop-outside-lock, reservation-failure cleanup, admitted Start, and overlapping same-id Discard regressions.
- Regression risk: replacing the existing APIs wholesale could break Phase-2 callers that intentionally want only ACTIVE states, while taking the controller lock across filesystem listing/sweeping would create unnecessary lock hold time. Snapshot only the ids under `_lock`, release it, then perform filesystem work; keep the narrower observer for its existing callers.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — this is the unclosed protection-consumer edge of round 30's Task-6.3 custody fix, not a new subsystem.

#### LEG 1 verification (executor `stage-6`, 2026-08-11, folded into this verify-then-fix leg per the practitioner's disposition)
- PR-MED-001: **real=yes; materiality=behavioral; severity=MED (agrees — the rounds-27/29/30 reachability grading); scope=IN-SCOPE; classification Fix-induced agreed** — the split-read union was written by the round-30 repair at exactly these two consumer sites. Confirmed against the code: `sweep_protected_ids` composed THREE separately-locked controller reads (`active_session_ids()`, `reserved_session_ids()`, `session`) and `_live_session_ids` TWO; each read is individually correct, but a Discard(X)-reserve plus admitted Start(Y) interleaved BETWEEN reads yields a returned set naming Y and omitting still-reserved X — a non-linearizable snapshot presented as THE protection source, re-opening the round-30 exposure for the reservation's lifetime.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Applied the practitioner-approved atomic-snapshot fix exactly as Desired behaviour prescribes. (1) NEW `SessionController.custody_protected_ids()` — every custody-protected id (all reservation keys PLUS the current non-terminal live session id, active states being a subset of non-terminal) constructed inside a SINGLE `_lock` acquisition; ids only under the lock, filesystem work after (docstring records the round-31 lesson: composing the union from separate public reads was itself a race). (2) BOTH external consumers now delegate to that one snapshot: `app.sweep_protected_ids` is `controller.custody_protected_ids() | extra` (nothing else read), and `MainWindow._live_session_ids` returns the snapshot directly — which also covers `RecoveryScreen._selection_blocked`, the provider's only other consumer. `active_session_ids()` is PRESERVED untouched for its Phase-2 callers, and `reserved_session_ids()` remains as the reservation-only diagnostic view with its docstring redirected to the snapshot. Both overclaiming docstrings corrected to state the atomic-snapshot rationale. Protocol (`SessionControllerLike`) + `FakeController` gained `custody_protected_ids` (the fake mirrors the union semantics). (3) Verification, one honest note on shape: the fix makes an INTRA-call pause point structurally unrepresentable — the whole union is built under one lock acquisition, so there is no "after the reservation read" boundary left to instrument inside the new method. The tests therefore pin the invariant from every angle that remains observable: `test_sweep_protected_ids_is_one_atomic_snapshot` and `test_live_session_ids_is_one_atomic_snapshot` are split-read POISON probes — controller stand-ins whose `reserved_session_ids`/`active_session_ids`/`session` reads fail the test loudly, proving each consumer performs exactly ONE snapshot call and composes nothing (deterministically RED against the pre-fix composition, which is the old split-read boundary made mechanical); the round-30 pointer-swap barrier now additionally asserts `{X, Y} ⊆ custody_protected_ids()` while Discard(X) is parked and Y is live (the real-controller linearizable state the split read could misreport); `test_expiry_sweep_skips_reserved_discard_target` runs an ACTUAL `sweep_sessions` off the atomic snapshot (reserved directory `skipped_active` with custody intact; released → `expired`, transient by construction); and `test_reservations_for_different_targets_are_independent` pins the two-simultaneous-reservations/release-one case in the snapshot. RETAINED untouched: all round-27/29/30 barriers, worker-stop-outside-lock, reservation-failure `finally` cleanup, admitted Start, and overlapping same-id Discard regressions (the round-30 UI stale-click and listing tests pass unchanged through the fake's mirrored snapshot). Composer verification pending (verify=composer); expected pytest 1182 + 4 = 1186.

- Primary baseline: HEAD `b57ca6f` (the Phase 5 commit) against the CURRENT uncommitted Phase 6 + round-27/28/29/30/31-fix tree. Static review only by explicit operator instruction; no suite or executable probe was run. Composer verification supplied by the operator: ruff clean, mypy strict 28 source files, pytest 1186 passed.
- Files reviewed: the current Phase 6 diffs and custody-critical call paths in `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/session.py`, `app.py`, `ui/main_window.py`, `ui/models.py`, `ui/recovery.py`, `note.py`, `session_store.py`, and `transcription.py`, plus the Phase 6 pipeline/session/store/transcription/UI tests. The custody-surface scan covered every shipping call to `unwrap_key_from_file`, `complete_session`, `discard_session`, `delete_session_key`, `sweep_sessions`, and `SessionCrypto.destroy`.
- Canonical plan/sidecar pre-check: PASS — both pair signals are present; the plan had 31 unique Findings Log round identities and the sidecar had 17; all 17 compacted plan rounds and sidecar rounds matched bidirectionally; the companion header, marker filenames, declared finding counts, finding IDs, and final decisions resolved one-to-one. Round 32 is the required next number.
- Round-31 fix confirmation: CONFIRMED. `SessionController.custody_protected_ids()` constructs the whole union — every positive reservation key plus the current non-terminal live id — inside one `_lock` critical section (`session.py:702-721`). `app.sweep_protected_ids()` and `MainWindow._live_session_ids()` each delegate to that method exactly once (`app.py:105-126`; `ui/main_window.py:130-143`), and `RecoveryScreen._selection_blocked()` reuses the latter provider at click time (`ui/recovery.py:175-186`). The controller lock is released before every filesystem listing or sweep. At the method's possible linearization points, X is present as the current non-terminal live session before reservation, as both live/reserved during reservation, and as reserved after admitted Start installs Y; X can disappear only after the last reservation release, which follows Discard's custody action. Therefore Discard(X)→Start(Y) cannot make a returned controller snapshot omit still-reserved X.
- Rounds 27/28/29 quick re-confirmation: their point fixes remain sound. Generation acquisition and live Complete consume `_custody_reservations` under `_lock`; every Discard reservation is acquired after refusal paths and released in the outer `finally`; provider/base-draft confinement plus finalisation's proposal-resolution route remain intact; and `write_note()` still canonicalises, refuses blocking warnings, and re-verifies shown-text/config/session/transcript bindings before its sole atomic write. Round 29's broader class-closed enumeration does not survive the inverse transcription interleaving below.
- Final custody-surface scan: no reserved target can be swept, listed, checked out, unwrapped through the shipping recovery runner, or mutated by a recovered coordinator operation before its reservation clears. Recovered Complete/Discard resolve and refuse a reserved identity while holding the same controller lock through mutation; identity-less crypto destruction refuses coarsely; the stale-list Resume and list-Discard paths re-check the atomic provider before unwrap/mutation. No new deadlock, strand, reservation leak, early-clear, or regression of admitted Start/second-Discard was found; no lock is held across `worker.stop()`, recovery listing, or expiry sweeping. The sole survivor is the live transcription consumer omitted from the reservation protocol below.
- Finding verification: 6 candidates; 5 dropped; 0 downgraded. Dropped after trace: a residual split protected-id read (both consumers make one snapshot call); per-id early-clear/leak (counts pair under the same lock and every post-reservation exit crosses `finally`); recovered-operation/unwrap reachability (atomic click re-check plus in-controller identity refusal); sweep/list reachability (X belongs to every relevant snapshot until release); and a new lock/join or admitted Start/second-Discard regression (none found). The surviving candidate is the exact inverse of the existing `transcribing` guard: Discard reserves first, then transcription begins in its unlocked window.
- Round classification: 0 Pre-existing / 0 Fix-induced / 1 Same-family. The underlying one-sided guard predates Phase 6, but this is the same reservation-consumer/class-closure family rounds 27, 29, and 30 were intended to exhaust.

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/session.py:464-478,549-616` — transcription can start after Discard reserves custody
- Classification: Same-family
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: `SessionController` promises all controls are safe from any thread (`session.py:250-253`), and the Discard guard's own comment says destroying the key under a live transcriber is wrong-by-contract (`:561-570`). The guard covers only transcribe-first ordering. In the inverse valid interleaving, a PROCESSING session has `transcribing == False`; thread A passes that check, reserves X, and releases `_lock` (`:549-593`); thread B then enters `transcribe()`, whose locked entry checks only state and `live.transcribing`, sets the flag, and begins using X's directory/crypto outside the lock (`:464-479`) without consulting the reservation; thread A reacquires `_lock` and calls key-first `discard_session()`, destroying that same crypto while the new transcriber is live (`:594-608`). The transcriber will normally fail closed before a durable transcript survives, and shipped GUI controls disable Discard while PROCESSING, so — consistently with the prior round-42 MED-003 and rounds-27/29/30 reachability grading — this is MED rather than HIGH. It still falsifies the any-thread custody contract and can leave an in-flight operation holding already-decrypted clinical buffers after Discard reports cryptographic deletion.
- Current behaviour: `discard()` tests `live.transcribing` only before its unlocked interval; `_custody_reservations` is consumed by generation acquisition, live Complete, recovered coordinator operations, snapshot consumers, and recovered-key destruction, but not by `transcribe()`.
- Desired behaviour: make Discard reservation and transcription acquisition mutually exclusive under `_lock` in both orders. Transcribe-first must keep making Discard refuse; Discard-first must make `transcribe()` refuse before invoking the transcriber or touching session crypto/store state. Preserve unrelated-session behaviour and keep the long transcriber call outside `_lock`.
- Pattern to follow: the round-27/29 consumer shape already used by `begin_generation()` and `complete()` — consult the controller-owned reservation while holding `_lock`, before installing an in-flight flag or crossing an unlocked boundary. Prefer an identity-scoped check against the PROCESSING live session id if avoiding coarse over-blocking is useful.
- Pattern siblings: the existing `test_discard_refused_while_transcribing` covers only transcribe-first ordering; add the inverse barrier beside it. No other surviving custody consumer gap was found in the final surface scan.
- Invariant: once Discard reserves session X, no transcription of X may begin or obtain X's crypto/store handle until that reservation is released; if transcription acquired first, Discard must refuse before reserving or deleting custody.
- Verification: add a deterministic two-thread rendezvous that parks Discard(X) after reservation on a PROCESSING session, attempts `transcribe()` from the other thread, and proves refusal occurs before the supplied transcriber callable runs and before any transcript/store mutation. Release Discard and prove it alone destroys X key-first and clears the reservation. Retain the existing transcribe-first refusal test, rounds-27/29/30/31 barriers, reservation-failure cleanup, worker-stop-outside-lock, admitted Start, and overlapping Discard regressions.
- Regression risk: a broad reservation check may transiently block transcription of a different live session Y while Discard(X) is parked after admitted Start; an identity-scoped check avoids that but must read the live snapshot and reservation map in the same critical section. Do not hold `_lock` across the transcriber call or `worker.stop()`.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — Task 6.3 owns the any-thread custody coordinator and this is a same-family missing consumer of the reservation introduced for its two-lock Discard transition.

#### LEG 1 verification (executor `stage-6`, 2026-08-12, folded into this verify-then-fix leg per the practitioner's disposition)
- PR-MED-001: **real=yes; materiality=behavioral; severity=MED (agrees — the rounds-27/29/30/31 reachability grading); scope=IN-SCOPE; Same-family classification agreed.** Confirmed against the code: `transcribe()`'s locked entry checked only `_require_state(PROCESSING)` and `live.transcribing` — never `_custody_reservations` — so in the inverse of the round-42 guard's order, a PROCESSING-session Discard could reserve, release `_lock`, and a `transcribe()` entering the window would install its flag and hand X's directory/crypto to the transcriber outside the lock, with Discard's second section then destroying that crypto beneath it. The guard was one-sided; the reservation had every consumer except this one.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Applied the round-27/29 consumer shape exactly as prescribed, IDENTITY-SCOPED per the finding's own regression note: `transcribe()`'s locked entry now checks `live.session.session_id in self._custody_reservations` — the live snapshot and the reservation map read in the SAME critical section, so a different session Y installed by an admitted Start is never transiently blocked by X's reservation — refusing with a typed `SessionActivityError` BEFORE the `transcribing` flag is installed, before the transcriber callable is invoked, and before any crypto/store use; the long transcriber call stays outside `_lock` (unchanged), and transcribe-first still makes Discard refuse via the existing `transcribing` guard — mutual exclusion now holds in BOTH orders. Verification added as prescribed: `test_transcribe_refused_inside_discard_unlocked_window`, the deterministic inverse two-thread rendezvous (event barriers, no sleeps): Discard(X) of a PROCESSING session parks with the reservation held; `transcribe()` from the other thread refuses with the callable proven un-run and the key intact at that instant; the released discard alone reaches DISCARDED key-first with the reservation clearing. The existing transcribe-first refusal, rounds-27/29/30/31 barriers, reservation-failure cleanup, worker-stop-outside-lock, admitted Start, and overlapping-Discard regressions all retained untouched. HONEST-RESIDUE DOCUMENTATION applied per the practitioner's explicit decision (the reason the pass converges here): the `SessionController` class docstring no longer claims blanket any-thread safety — it states the ACCURATE contract (custody operations serialized through the lock + the per-session reservation, covering discard's window in both orders, sufficient for the shipped single-GUI-thread queued-signal usage) and NAMES the residue (full arbitrary-thread custody safety deferred to a future dedicated holistic serialization hardening; the six MED races of rounds 27–32 each needed a non-GUI-thread custody caller that shipped wiring does not have; do not add one without that hardening — candidate Phase-8 threat-model item); the round-42 discard-guard comment corrected to reference the stated contract and the now-two-sided mutual exclusion. Residue note also added to the plan's Phase 6 handoff block. **PEER PASS CONVERGED BY PRACTITIONER DECISION: fix + honest-residue documentation, NO round 33.** Composer verification pending (verify=composer); expected pytest 1186 + 1 = 1187.
