#### MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/main_window.py:217` (`_on_recovered`) + `ui/recovery.py` `set_generation_blocked` — the residue guard leaves an in-flight recovery's view swap reachable during generation
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast (localized UI wiring; no custody-coordinator change)
- Why it matters: Phase 6 handed Phase 7 the explicit mandate to keep view swaps UNREACHABLE during generation, or a refused `_destroy_recovered_crypto` reference can be overwritten unzeroized. `set_generation_blocked` disables recovery BUTTONS (blocks a NEW resume start during a lease) but does not stop a resume that was ALREADY in flight when the lease was acquired. That resume's completion fires `_on_recovered` → `transcript_screen.show_document(...)` → `_reset_generation_state()` → `_release_lease()`, releasing the lease mid-review and swapping the view — silently discarding the in-progress note and dropping the lease's custody protection while the compose worker may still hold the queued session's `(directory, crypto)`; in the narrow sub-case where a recovered key is already retained, `_on_recovered`'s `_recovered_crypto = crypto` overwrites it unzeroized.
- Current behaviour: Generate can be clicked while a recovery resume is in flight; the resume completing then releases the held lease and swaps the view.
- Desired behaviour: a `GenerationLease` is never held concurrently with a recovery resume, so `_on_recovered` (fired only when a resume completes) can never run while a lease is held.
- Pattern to follow: the existing mutual-exclusion posture — `set_generation_blocked` already blocks a resume STARTING during a lease; add the symmetric guard blocking a generation STARTING during a resume (a click-time refusal like `recovery._selection_blocked`).
- Invariant: no `GenerationLease` is held while `RecoveryScreen.is_busy` is True; therefore `_on_recovered` never runs while `TranscriptScreen.is_busy`.
- Verification: offscreen test `test_generate_refused_while_recovery_in_flight` — with `recovery_busy_provider` returning True, `generate()` acquires no lease and surfaces a message (Generate also disabled); with it False, generation proceeds unchanged.
- Regression risk: the transcript screen's Generate enablement and the main-window construction wiring. Mitigated by the default `recovery_busy_provider=None` (no gating), so existing transcript tests are unchanged.
- Severity rationale (MED not HIGH): reachability needs two concurrent heavy-ML flows (a recovery resume plus a live session reaching QUEUED); the unzeroized-key sub-case is largely blocked by the checkout-exclusion invariant (a checked-out recovery blocks a second resume, so `_recovered_crypto` is normally None when a fresh `_on_recovered` fires); and no wrong-session data loss occurs (a QUEUED session's Complete/Discard/Start are all UI-unavailable after the swap). In-scope (Phase 7's residue mandate), Fix-now, non-production → headless AUTO-DISPOSABLE.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Added `recovery_busy_provider: Callable[[], bool] | None` to `TranscriptScreen`; `_update_controls` disables Generate while a recovery is in flight and `generate()` re-checks it at click time (fail-closed, like the recovery screen's stale-selection re-check) and refuses with a message before acquiring the lease. `MainWindow` passes `recovery_busy_provider=self._recovery_in_flight` (returns `recovery_screen.is_busy`). Combined with the existing `set_generation_blocked` (resume refused during a lease), the two flows are now mutually exclusive at their start points, so no lease is ever held while a recovery resume is in flight → `_on_recovered` can never fire during a lease → the view swap never releases a held lease and never overwrites a retained recovered key. Test `test_generate_refused_while_recovery_in_flight` added. Composer-VERIFIED green (round-34 resume): ruff clean, mypy strict 29 files, UI+lease suites 211 pass (+1, none regressed).
- /fix date: 2026-08-12
- /fix applied by: Claude Code (executor `stage-7`, round 33)

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/transcript.py:454` + `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/note.py:447` — failed "complete without note" releases the generation lease permanently while review state remains live
- Classification: Pre-existing
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: `abandon_note_and_complete()` releases the lease before calling `complete_without_note()`. That operation can fail after the note-removal attempt or during transcript fsync/decrypt verification, retaining the session key and leaving the controller QUEUED. `NoteScreen.abandon()` catches that failure and deliberately does not clear the review. The application is therefore still visibly mid-review, but the authoritative controller lease is already gone and `generation_active_changed(False)` has unblocked recovery. An unrelated recovery can now start and later swap the transcript view, clearing the retained review state; the original queued session has also lost the lease protection the UI still appears to rely on.
- Current behaviour: `TranscriptScreen._release_lease()` runs unconditionally before `controller.complete_without_note()`. There is no rollback or lease reacquisition if completion raises.
- Desired behaviour: the exact held lease remains authoritative until delete-note-and-complete succeeds. On failure, the same lease and review must remain held and recovery/view swaps must remain blocked.
- Pattern to follow: add a controller operation that consumes the exact `GenerationLease`, performs `complete_session(..., delete_note=True)` while that lease remains installed, and clears the lease only after success. A failure should leave the controller lease unchanged. The UI should clear its local lease and emit `generation_active_changed(False)` only after that operation succeeds.
- Pattern siblings: `save_note()` correctly releases only after `write_note()` returns; `complete()` and `complete_without_note()` already keep their state transition after successful custody verification.
- Invariant: a failed alternate terminal action must never turn an in-progress review into an unleased QUEUED session.
- Verification: traced `NoteScreen.abandon()` → `MainWindow._on_note_abandon()` → `TranscriptScreen.abandon_note_and_complete()` → `_release_lease()` → `SessionController.complete_without_note()`. Any exception from the final call returns to `NoteScreen.abandon()` before `clear()`, while the release signal has already unblocked recovery.
- Regression risk: combining lease consumption with completion must preserve key-retention-on-failure, note-unlink-first ordering, and the existing discard-reservation exclusion. Do not implement this as release-then-reacquire, which leaves a concurrency window.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — this is the explicit Phase 7 delete-note exit and directly violates the phase's lease-lifecycle invariant.
- LEG-1 verification (executor `stage-7`, round 35): real=yes; materiality=behavioral; severity=MED (agree — latent-until-a-completion-failure, but a real lease-lifecycle inversion); scope=IN-SCOPE; Pre-existing agreed. Evidence: `transcript.py:462` calls `self._release_lease()` before `controller.complete_without_note()` at `:463`; on a `complete_without_note` raise, `NoteScreen.abandon()` (`note.py:452-458`) catches and does NOT clear, so the review stays visible while the controller lease is already gone and recovery unblocked.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Changed `SessionController.complete_without_note` to CONSUME THE EXACT held lease: `complete_without_note(lease: GenerationLease)` requires `self._generation is lease` (identity, like `with_generation_custody`), keeps the existing `_custody_reservations` exclusion, runs `complete_session(..., delete_note=True)` UNDER the lock (the pre-existing `complete()`/`complete_without_note()` pattern — no new unlocked window), and clears `self._generation = None` ONLY after the WRITTEN transition — so a completion failure (raise) leaves `_generation` unchanged (lease held), the session QUEUED, and the key retained (note-unlink-first inside `complete_session`). `TranscriptScreen.abandon_note_and_complete()` now calls `controller.complete_without_note(lease)` FIRST and only `_clear()`s (releasing the local mirror + emitting `generation_active_changed(False)`) AFTER it returns — on failure it raises to `NoteScreen.abandon()` with the lease + review fully held, recovery still blocked. Protocol + `FakeController` updated (fake consumes the lease on success, raises `generation_error` to simulate failure). Tests: `test_completion_failure_keeps_the_lease` (corrupt-transcript → `complete_without_note(lease)` raises → lease still held, key retained, QUEUED), `test_deletes_note_and_completes` / `test_completes_without_a_note_present` rewritten to pass the held lease and assert it is consumed, `test_refused_with_foreign_lease` (a non-held lease is refused, key intact); the UI `test_abandon_note_and_complete_without_one` still green (fake success path). Composer verification pending.
- /fix date: 2026-08-12
- /fix applied by: Claude Code (executor `stage-7`, round 35)

#### PR-MED-002: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/note.py:461` — the copy-enabled branch permits copying a note with unresolved blocking errors
- Classification: Pre-existing
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: the shipping constant currently prevents exposure, so this is MED rather than HIGH. However, the intended `copy_enabled=True` outcome makes the button and text selection available solely from the shipping flag. `_copy_note()` checks only that flag and that a note exists. A note containing pending proposals, reconstruction errors, contradictions, or another unresolved `error` can therefore enter the Windows clipboard and be pasted into Cliniko as soon as Task 9.1 flips the recorded decision. That violates the critical constraint that unresolved errors block copy.
- Current behaviour: `_apply_copy_binding()` enables selection and the Copy button whenever `_copy_enabled` is true. `_copy_note()` performs no blocking-warning, saved-state, or acknowledgement check. `test_copy_enabled_when_gate_passed` constructs the ordinary pending-proposal result and explicitly expects Copy to be enabled, pinning the unsafe state.
- Desired behaviour: the shipping flag is necessary but not sufficient. Copy and text selection must also require a ratified review state: no pending proposal or blocking error, the note successfully saved, and—if copy shares Complete's ratification bar—no unacknowledged review warning. The click handler must re-check the same condition.
- Pattern to follow: derive copy enablement from one pure state predicate, analogous to `complete_block_reason`, and apply it to both `copy_button` and `note_body` interaction flags. Re-evaluate it after every resolution, Save, acknowledgement, and retract.
- Pattern siblings: `save_button` already gates on `blocking_warnings()`; `TranscriptScreen` merges the live lease into Complete's state at every update.
- Invariant: no note carrying an unresolved error may reach any clipboard path, even after the Task 9.1 feature flag becomes true.
- Verification: read `_copy_note()` and `_apply_copy_binding()` at `ui/note.py:461–482`, then checked the enabled-outcome test at `tests/test_ui_screens.py:1674`; no other copy-state guard exists.
- Regression risk: disabling only the button is insufficient because selectable text retains native copy shortcuts. Both interaction flags and the explicit button must use the same predicate; the always-display-only transcript panel must remain unchanged.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — Task 7.1 explicitly builds and tests both outcomes of the Task 9.1 copy binding.
- LEG-1 verification (executor `stage-7`, round 35): real=yes; materiality=behavioral (latent — the 9.1 flag is False today, but the enabled-path pins the unsafe state); severity=MED (agree — gated behind the flag); scope=IN-SCOPE; Pre-existing agreed. Evidence: `_apply_copy_binding` (`note.py:470-482`) sets note_body selectable + copy_button enabled on `_copy_enabled` alone; `_copy_note` (`:461-468`) checks only `_copy_enabled` + note-exists; `test_copy_enabled_when_gate_passed` asserts Copy enabled on the pending-proposal fixture — pinning the unsafe state.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Copy now shares Complete's ratification bar via one pure predicate. Added `_copy_ready()` = `self._copy_enabled and self._note is not None and models.complete_block_reason(self.current_review_state()) is None` — so copy is available ONLY when the 9.1 flag is on AND the note is fully ratified (no pending proposal, no blocking error, saved, no unacknowledged review — the same bar `complete_block_reason` enforces for Complete). `_apply_copy_binding` now derives BOTH the `note_body` interaction flags (selectable iff `_copy_ready()`, else `NoTextInteraction`) AND `copy_button.setEnabled(_copy_ready())` from it (button VISIBLE per the 9.1 flag, ENABLED per readiness); it is called from `_update_controls`, and `_acknowledge`/`_acknowledge_all` now also call `_update_controls` so acknowledgement re-derives readiness. `_copy_note()` re-checks `_copy_ready()` at click time (fail-closed). Test `test_copy_enabled_when_gate_passed` rewritten to `test_copy_enabled_only_for_a_ratified_note`: with the gate on, Copy stays DISABLED + note_body `NoTextInteraction` while proposals are pending; it becomes enabled + selectable only after confirm-all → acknowledge-all → save. `test_copy_disabled_when_gate_not_passed` / `test_default_copy_binding_ships_disabled` unchanged (flag off ⇒ never ready). Composer verification pending.

#### PR-MED-003: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/models.py:448` + `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/note.py:422` — blocking provider-output errors advertise a Regenerate/retract clear-path that the UI cannot perform
- Classification: Pre-existing
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: `source_coords_invalid` and `reconstruction_mismatch` can attach to provider/base transcript assertions. Those assertions have no proposal row, so they cannot be retracted. Their warning copy tells the clinician to "Regenerate the note, or retract the affected line," but no Regenerate/cancel-review control exists. Generate remains disabled because the current lease is held, and the only available escape is the destructive "Delete note and complete without one," which destroys the session key and eliminates the promised regeneration route. The checker fails closed, but the named recovery path is fictitious.
- Current behaviour: proposal controls are built only from `draft.note_proposals`; base assertions are display-only. The action area contains Save, delete-and-complete, and gated Copy. `TranscriptScreen` disables Generate while `_lease` is held, and no callback releases the draft without also completing the session.
- Desired behaviour: provide a visible, non-destructive Regenerate/cancel-review action that discards only the in-memory draft, releases the lease deliberately, retains the QUEUED transcript/key, returns to the Transcript screen, and makes Generate available again. Warning copy should name controls that actually exist.
- Pattern to follow: add a dedicated Note-screen callback to a `TranscriptScreen.cancel_note_review()` operation, distinct from `abandon_note_and_complete()`. It should reset `_generation_result` and review state, release the held lease, clear plaintext, and switch back to the Transcript tab without touching `note.enc`, transcript custody, or session state.
- Pattern siblings: compose failure already releases the lease while leaving the transcript available for retry; the new user-driven regenerate path should provide the same custody outcome after a draft has been presented.
- Invariant: every blocking warning displayed to the clinician has at least one visible, non-destructive clear-path when regeneration can resolve it.
- Verification: traced the warning copy at `ui/models.py:448–478`, proposal-row construction at `ui/note.py:253–296`, the complete action set at `ui/note.py:422–482`, and Generate enablement at `ui/transcript.py:268–284`. No base-assertion retract or regenerate callback exists.
- Regression risk: the regenerate action must not release the lease before the compose worker has finished, must not delete `note.enc` or the session key, and must clear both transcript-side review state and Note-tab plaintext so a stale note cannot later re-enable Complete.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — Task 7.1 requires each blocking state to name its clear-path, and Flow 2 promises Regenerate as the alternative to deleting the note and completing.
- LEG-1 verification (executor `stage-7`, round 35): real=yes; materiality=behavioral; severity=MED (agree); scope=IN-SCOPE; Pre-existing agreed. Evidence: base transcript assertions are display-only (no proposal row — `note.py` `_build_proposal_rows` iterates `draft.note_proposals` only); `source_coords_invalid`/`reconstruction_mismatch` copy names "Regenerate the note, or retract the affected line" (`models.py` `WARNING_COPY`); Generate is disabled while `_lease` is held (`transcript.py` `_update_controls`); the only escape was the destructive abandon. Cancel is reachable only AFTER the draft is presented (Note tab shown ⇒ compose worker already returned), so releasing the lease at cancel is safe.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Added a NON-DESTRUCTIVE `TranscriptScreen.cancel_note_review()`: it `_release_lease()`s (deliberate — the compose worker has already returned by the time the Note tab shows a draft), resets `_generation_result` and `_note_review_state = NoteReviewState()`, and `_update_controls()` (re-enabling Generate), WITHOUT touching `note.enc`, transcript custody, or session state — the QUEUED transcript + key are retained for regeneration. The Note tab gained a `Cancel review and regenerate` button wired to a new `on_cancel` callback (`begin_review(..., on_cancel=...)`, default None); `cancel_review()` calls it then `clear()`s the Note-tab plaintext. `MainWindow._on_note_cancel` calls `transcript.cancel_note_review()` and switches back to the Transcript tab; `_on_draft_ready` passes `on_cancel=self._on_note_cancel`. The cancel button is enabled only while a draft is under review and not yet saved (pre-commit escape). Warning copy in `models.WARNING_COPY` updated to name controls that ACTUALLY exist: `source_coords_invalid`/`reconstruction_mismatch`/`role_unconfirmed` now say "Cancel the review and regenerate the note" (+ the role one names confirming the clinician on the Transcript screen); `contradiction`/`autofill_trigger_absent` name declining the proposed line OR cancel-and-regenerate. Tests: `test_cancel_note_review_is_non_destructive` (transcript-level: lease released, `_generation_result` cleared, no complete/discard/complete_without_note call, Generate re-enabled) and `test_cancel_review_calls_callback_and_clears` (Note-level). Composer verification pending.
- /fix date: 2026-08-12
- /fix applied by: Claude Code (executor `stage-7`, round 35)

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/note.py:560` + `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/transcript.py:454` — a stale saved Note tab can consume the next generation's live lease

- Classification: Pre-existing / missed by round 35
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: after Save, the lease is released and Generate becomes available, but the old Note tab and its enabled delete-and-complete callback remain live. During a second compose worker, that stale action reads the new mutable `_lease`; the controller's exact-identity check therefore succeeds and destroys the session key while the new worker is still using or about to use the same custody.
- Current behaviour: `save_note()` releases the first lease at `transcript.py:452`; `NoteScreen._update_controls()` leaves `abandon_button` enabled after Save at `note.py:560`; a second `generate()` installs a new lease and runs `with_generation_custody()` outside the lock; the stale callback then reaches `abandon_note_and_complete()`, which reads and consumes that new lease. The worker can subsequently fail on destroyed crypto or emit a late draft after the controller is already WRITTEN. Immediately after Save, before another generation begins, the same enabled button instead fails with "no note generation is in progress."
- Desired behaviour: a review action may act only on the generation/review instance that created it. No stale widget may obtain a later lease, and delete-note-and-complete must be refused while a compose action is running.
- Pattern to follow: bind Note-tab callbacks to an immutable generation/review identity, invalidate or disable the previous Note tab synchronously when a new generation starts, and reject callbacks whose identity is not the currently presented completed draft. Consider a controller-side "review ready" phase/capability so lease identity alone cannot authorise completion before that lease's worker has returned.
- Pattern siblings: `with_generation_custody()` correctly identity-checks its token; the missing relation is between that token and the particular presented `NoteGenerationResult`.
- Invariant: no terminal custody transition may consume a lease while its compose worker is running, and no action from review A may operate on review B's lease.
- Verification: static sequence: save A → switch to Transcript → start generation B → switch to the still-populated Note tab → click its enabled delete-and-complete button. `MainWindow._on_note_abandon()` carries no generation identity, and `TranscriptScreen.abandon_note_and_complete()` reads B's current lease. Existing tests exercise only one generation and cannot expose this interleaving.
- Fix recommendation: add a blocking-worker regression test for the sequence above, asserting the stale action is disabled/refused, B's lease and key remain held, and no late worker signal can reopen a draft after terminal completion. Preserve a separate guarded delete-saved-note path for the post-Save/no-lease state.
- Regression risk: changing this lifecycle must preserve legitimate pre-Save delete-and-complete, post-Save regeneration, completion-failure lease retention, and the recovery-generation exclusion.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — this is the shipped Phase 7 lease/custody path and the explicit delete-note exit.
- Severity rationale: MED because it requires Save, explicit regeneration, tab switching, and an explicit destructive click; it does not silently write unsupported content, but it breaks a custody invariant in reachable shipped UI.
- LEG-1 verification (executor `stage-7`, round 36): real=yes; materiality=behavioral (custody-invariant break in reachable shipped UI); severity=MED (agree — requires Save + regenerate + tab switch + destructive click); scope=IN-SCOPE; Pre-existing/missed-by-round-35 agreed. Evidence: `note.py:560` `abandon_button.setEnabled(self._on_abandon is not None)` — no `not _note_saved` gate, so abandon stays enabled after Save; `transcript.py abandon_note_and_complete` reads the mutable `self._lease`; the Note tab is a single reused widget, so after Save A its stale abandon routes through B's new lease.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Two-part structural fix. (1) INVALIDATE the stale Note tab: `MainWindow._on_generation_active(True)` now `note_screen.clear()`s synchronously when a generation STARTS, so no stale (post-Save) Note tab survives into a new generation — the stale abandon is gone before B's compose (its `_on_abandon` is None → `abandon()` is a no-op, and the button is disabled). A new generation's own Note tab appears only AFTER `draft_ready` (compose worker returned), so no terminal action can fire while a compose worker runs. (2) Route the POST-SAVE delete path OFF the lease: `TranscriptScreen.abandon_note_and_complete()` now routes by `self._lease` — pre-save (lease held) → `complete_without_note(lease)` (round-35 leased path); post-save (no lease, note committed) → the NEW non-leased `SessionController.complete_deleting_saved_note()` (the pre-round-35 shape: refused while a generation lease is held OR a discard is completing, `complete_session(delete_note=True)` under the lock, note-unlink-first, key-retention-on-failure). So the post-Save "delete note and complete without one" WORKS (previously raised "no note generation is in progress") and can never consume a live lease. Protocol + `FakeController` extended. Tests: `test_new_generation_invalidates_stale_note_tab` (the peer's sequence — a new generation clears the stale Note tab, its abandon action gone), `TestCompleteDeletingSavedNote` controller tests (deletes + completes; refused while generating; failure keeps the key). Composer verification pending.
- /fix date: 2026-08-12
- /fix applied by: Claude Code (executor `stage-7`, round 36)

#### PR-MED-002: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/transcript.py:484` + `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/models.py:607` — canceling a replacement draft forgets an existing `note.enc` and bypasses Complete acknowledgement gating

- Classification: Fix-induced by round 35 PR-MED-003
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: Save intentionally permits review warnings to remain unacknowledged; acknowledgement gates Complete, not `write_note`. If the clinician starts regeneration and cancels the replacement draft, the earlier saved artifact remains on disk while the UI resets to `has_note=False`. Complete is then enabled and can cryptographically complete that earlier note without its required warning acknowledgement.
- Current behaviour: save note A, leaving a review warning unacknowledged; generate draft B; click Cancel review and regenerate. `cancel_note_review()` leaves `note.enc` A untouched but assigns `NoteReviewState()`. `complete_block_reason()` returns `None` when `has_note` is false, so Complete becomes enabled. `complete_session()` verifies A's cryptographic/session binding but has no acknowledgement state to enforce, then deletes the key.
- Desired behaviour: canceling a replacement review must reconcile the still-existing committed note. It must restore A's visible review and acknowledgement state, or keep Complete blocked until the clinician explicitly chooses to retain or delete A. "No note" may be asserted only when no current `note.enc` exists.
- Pattern to follow: preserve a generation-scoped committed-note snapshot/state while regenerating and restore it on Cancel, or introduce an explicit artifact-state transition that removes the prior note before treating the session as note-free. The non-destructive Cancel contract rules out silently deleting A.
- Pattern siblings: compose failure already releases the new lease while retaining the preceding review state; Cancel should produce the same rollback semantics when a committed note predates the attempted regeneration.
- Invariant: the Complete gate's `has_note` state must agree with the current encrypted artifact, and no saved note with unacknowledged review warnings may be completed.
- Verification: `generate()` remains enabled after Save and does not remove `note.enc`; starting B does not invoke `write_transcript`; Cancel explicitly does not touch `note.enc`; the reset at `transcript.py:485-486` loses all knowledge of A. Existing Cancel tests start from a session with no prior saved note and therefore miss this case.
- Fix recommendation: add an end-to-end UI/controller test for save-A-with-unacknowledged-review → generate B → cancel B, asserting A is restored or Complete remains blocked and the artifact cannot be silently completed.
- Regression risk: preserve first-generation Cancel's non-destructive behaviour, Generate re-enablement, and the rule that a failed/canceled replacement never destroys the session key.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — this is the new round-35 Cancel path and the Phase 7 Complete-gating invariant.
- Severity rationale: MED because the earlier artifact has already passed blocking-error and write validation, limiting the failure to skipped mandatory review acknowledgement and stale-intent handling; the path is nevertheless fully reachable through ordinary UI actions.
- LEG-1 verification (executor `stage-7`, round 36): real=yes; materiality=behavioral (bypasses the plan's acknowledgement precondition); severity=MED (agree); scope=IN-SCOPE; fix-induced-by-round-35 agreed. Evidence: `note.py` `save_button` gates on `blocking` only (NOT unacknowledged), so A can be saved with an unacknowledged review — but this DEVIATES from plan Flow 1 ("Zero unresolved error and zero unacknowledged review warnings are preconditions" of finalisation); `cancel_note_review` resets `_note_review_state = NoteReviewState()` (has_note=False) while `note.enc` A persists, and the Complete gate reads only that UI state.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Two-part fix aligning with plan Flow 1. (1) SAVE NOW REQUIRES ACKNOWLEDGEMENT (the plan's stated finalisation precondition — round 35 wrongly gated it on Complete only): `NoteScreen.save_button` and `save()` both refuse while any review warning is unacknowledged (in addition to pending proposals / blocking errors). So a saved `note.enc` is ALWAYS fully ratified (no unacknowledged review can ever be committed) — completing it later can never bypass acknowledgement, which closes the safety core of this finding at the source. (2) The Complete gate now AGREES WITH DISK: `TranscriptScreen` tracks `_note_committed` (set on a successful `save_note`, reset in `_reset_generation_state` = new session / terminal), and `_update_controls` merges it into the gate — `has_note = state.has_note or _note_committed`, `note_saved = state.note_saved or _note_committed` — so after Cancel-of-B the gate reports has_note=True (a committed note.enc A exists), never the false "no note". Cancel stays non-destructive (A is not deleted); because A was ratified at Save (acknowledged), completing A after cancelling B is safe and correct, and the "no note" state is asserted only when no note.enc exists. Tests: `test_committed_note_survives_cancel_of_replacement` (committed A → generate B → cancel B → `_note_committed` retained, Complete gate has_note=True, A completable), `test_save_requires_acknowledgement` (Note-tab: save disabled with an unacknowledged review). Existing `test_save_calls_callback_when_clean` → `test_save_calls_callback_when_ratified` and `test_confirming_all_clears_blocking_and_enables_save` → `test_confirming_all_then_acknowledge_enables_save` updated to acknowledge before saving. Composer verification pending.
- /fix date: 2026-08-12
- /fix applied by: Claude Code (executor `stage-7`, round 36)

  - **PR-MED-001 — CONFIRMED sound and complete.** `TranscriptScreen.generate()` installs the controller lease, assigns its local mirror, and emits `generation_active_changed(True)` before constructing or starting the compose task (`ui/transcript.py:397-422`). In the same GUI-thread call stack, `MainWindow._on_generation_active(True)` blocks recovery and clears the Note tab (`ui/main_window.py:315-327`). `NoteScreen.clear()` removes the note and nulls Save, Cancel, and Abandon callbacks before disabling their controls (`ui/note.py:231-259`), making every stale terminal action a no-op. The new Note tab appears only after `_on_composed()` has ended the worker and emitted `draft_ready` (`ui/transcript.py:424-435`). The post-Save delete path no longer consumes a lease and is independently refused while any later generation is active (`ui/transcript.py:475-502`; `session.py:620-644`).
  - **PR-MED-002 — CONFIRMED sound and complete.** Flow 1 explicitly makes zero unacknowledged review warnings a finalisation precondition. Save enablement checks this at `ui/note.py:558-571`, and `save()` repeats the check at click time at `ui/note.py:436-447`. Pending proposals remain blocking errors through `finalise_note()` and `write_note()` (`note.py:1885-1934`; `session_store.py:786-821`). There is no intended partially-reviewed-note save path in the plan. Successful Save sets `_note_committed` only after `write_note` succeeds; Cancel of replacement draft B preserves that state, and Complete merges it with the current review state (`ui/transcript.py:105-109,266-310,458-473,508-520`). Because A could only have been saved after acknowledgement, completing A after cancelling B cannot bypass ratification.
- Standing safety invariants re-verified:
  - The generation lease spans compose → review → GUI-thread write and is released only after successful write or deliberate cleanup.
  - `shown_text_digest` is derived from the PlainText proposal widget and independently matched during finalisation and write.
  - Recovery/generation exclusion remains symmetric, with no view-swap window in shipped GUI-thread wiring.
  - Complete is gated authoritatively: unresolved review state retains the lease; cancellation can release it only to no note or an already-ratified committed note.
  - Copy requires the shipping gate and fully ratified state at both enablement and click time; disabled text remains non-selectable.
  - No reviewed UI path logs clinical text.
  - Provider output remains confined to transcript-provenance draft assertions; clinician-authored assertions require a matching emitted proposal and confirmed resolution.
  - The new leased and non-leased completion operations preserve note-unlink-first, transcript verification, key-retention-on-failure, exact-lease identity, and discard-reservation exclusion.

- Source: Codex peer-review (`gpt-5.6-sol`); verify-then-fix by executor `stage-8` under `gates=fix-biased` (headless auto-dispose)

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/models.py:329-331` - the new consent reminder overstates what is never written and understates the checkbox attestation
- Classification: Fix-induced (Round 45 MED-001 / Task 7.7)
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: this is the manual safety instruction for a legal attestation. It currently says, "If consent was gained, tick the Informed Consent box yourself," but the ratified constraint records that the checkbox attests that the working diagnosis, benefits and risks were explained **and** consent was gained. Reducing that predicate to consent alone can prompt a clinician to tick a stronger claim than the reminder asked them to verify. Its opening sentence is also overbroad: the app can write a transcript-derived canonical `Consent` section (`note.py:816-817,1066-1108`), render it in the note, and a custom profile may map `consent` to a free-text target; what is structurally unreachable is specifically an `attestation_checkbox` target.
- Current behaviour: `CONSENT_MANUAL_REMINDER` says "Informed Consent is never written, ticked, or proposed by this app" and conditions manual ticking only on consent having been gained. The surrounding implementation correctly keeps the reminder unconditional and prevents mappings to attestation-typed targets, but the user-facing words blur the distinction between the free-text Consent section and the checkbox.
- Desired behaviour: name the **Informed Consent attestation checkbox** as the thing the app never writes, ticks, or proposes, and tell the practitioner to verify the full Cliniko attestation (working diagnosis, benefits and risks explained, and consent gained) before ticking it manually. Do not imply that the canonical free-text Consent section can never contain note text.
- Pattern to follow: `note_config.py:55-60,245-246` precisely scopes the structural rule to an attestation-typed target; the full predicate is recorded at plan Critical Constraint line 310.
- Pattern siblings: the Task-7.7 test currently pins presence, unconditional rendering, survival across `clear()`, and absence from warning codes; extend the semantic pin so the copy cannot regress to consent-alone or lose the checkbox/attestation distinction.
- Invariant: the app must never create a consent attestation, and its manual reminder must never invite the clinician to attest more than the reminder tells them to verify.
- Verification: traced the exact reminder text into `NoteScreen.consent_reminder_label`, then traced `ExtractiveNoteProvider._route` and `DEFAULT_SECTION_CUES["consent"]`, `format_note_body`, `TemplateProfile._check_profile`, and the ratified constraint. The code forbids the checkbox target, not all consent-related note text.
- Regression risk: preserve the Round-45 implementation properties - static, unconditional, non-acknowledgeable, and not cleared with note plaintext. The fix is wording plus a focused UI-model assertion; it must not weaken the target-type construction guard.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope - this corrects the user-facing safety copy introduced by the immediately preceding Task 7.7 fix.
- /fix decision: **Applied**
- /fix notes: CONFIRMED on both halves. (a) `plan:310` records the checkbox as attesting that the working diagnosis, benefits and risks were explained AND consent was gained; the round-45 copy asked the clinician to verify consent alone and then invited the tick - inviting a stronger claim than it asked them to verify, the precise failure the constraint exists to prevent. (b) `_RAW_SECTION_CUES` at `note.py:816-817` routes consent speech ("happy to proceed", "explained the risks") into the canonical `consent` section, which `format_note_body` renders, and `note_config`'s docstring records that another profile may legitimately map `consent` to a FREE-TEXT target - so "never written" was overbroad; what is structurally unreachable is an `attestation_checkbox` TARGET. Reworded to name the attestation checkbox, carry the full predicate, and say explicitly that a rendered Consent section is not the attestation. The two-requirement rationale is recorded inline above the constant so the next editor cannot regress it silently. Round-45 implementation properties preserved (static, unconditional, non-acknowledgeable, survives `clear()`), and the target-type construction guard untouched. Test extended per the peer's Pattern-siblings note: the semantic pin now asserts "attestation" plus each of working diagnosis / benefits / risks / consent-was-gained, so consent-alone regression fails.
- /fix date: 2026-08-18
- /fix applied by: live Claude-family executor (`claude-opus-5`), `/peer-loop` round 47 fix leg

#### PR-LOW-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/app.py:9`; `session_store.py:842`; `ui/models.py:223-224`; `ui/recovery.py:258-260` - hard-cap and recovery-assurance claims survive the Round-45 cadence fix
- Classification: Same-family (Round 45 LOW-001; rounds 40-43 retention-claim class)
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: these comments and one clinician-facing failure message still state stronger retention/recovery guarantees than the shipped mechanisms provide. The periodic sweep is a best-effort GUI-thread timer; protected stores are exempt; an I/O error retains an otherwise-expired store; the recovery list intentionally lists conservatively when no timestamp is readable; and a recovery failure can itself mean the key or store is corrupt. The named Round-45 comment now says all of this correctly, but its siblings can still mislead maintainers and falsely reassure a clinician after an irrecoverable failure.
- Current behaviour: the `app.py` module docstring says the periodic sweep "keeps the cap enforced"; the `session_store.py` section header calls the 24-hour cap "enforced by code"; `list_recoverable_sessions` claims it will "never offer recovery" past the window despite its no-readable-timestamp conservative branch; and `_on_recovery_failed` says the session "remains recoverable within its 24-hour window" for every failure class.
- Desired behaviour: use the established precise model everywhere: an **unprotected** store becomes expiry-eligible at 24 hours and is destroyed by the next successful sweep; 15 minutes is cadence, not a deadline. Describe the listing's conservative no-readable-timestamp branch honestly, and make the failure message state only what is known (custody was not deliberately discarded; retry may or may not succeed depending on the error) rather than promising recoverability.
- Pattern to follow: the corrected `_SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS` comment at `app.py:27-38` and `docs/security/retention-schedule.md`'s expiry-eligible/next-successful-sweep wording.
- Pattern siblings: retain the correctly scoped clock-skew claims in `earliest_trusted_timestamp` / `_session_created_at`; they describe that trust calculation rather than asserting a successful-sweep deadline. The Recovery heading's "24-hour window" may remain as policy framing if the adjacent copy no longer turns it into an absolute availability promise.
- Invariant: no code comment, docstring, or user-facing string may describe a QTimer cadence, a protected/exempt store, a failed I/O operation, or a failed recovery as a hard destruction or availability guarantee.
- Verification: followed `main.periodic_sweep` into `sweep_sessions`, including its `active_session_ids` exemption and `except OSError: action = "error"`; followed the listing's `elif readable` / no-readable fallbacks; and followed `TaskThread.failed` into the unconditional `_on_recovery_failed` message. The Round-45 interval comment is correct but these siblings are not.
- Regression risk: wording changes must not weaken the key-first deletion order or accidentally invite recovery after confirmed cryptographic deletion. Keep the distinction between expiry policy, successful enforcement, and recoverability.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope - direct same-family completeness check of the Round-45 code-comment fix and the phase's retention UI.
- /fix decision: **Applied** as a CLASS sweep, not four instances. A post-fix grep over `src/` for `24 h cap|24-hour window|cap enforced|keeps the cap|enforced by code|never offer|24 h rule` returns 11 hits: the 4 corrected, and 7 VERIFIED correct-as-written and deliberately retained - `session_store.py:855/881/906` describe the shared trust calculation and the clock-skew fail-safe (not a destruction deadline), `ui/recovery.py:105` is policy framing on a list heading, `note_config.py:11` says config sits OUTSIDE the rule, and `ui/transcript.py:540` is already conditional and was narrowed in round 42. So the peer's four sites were the complete class.
- /fix notes: (1) `app.py:9` "a periodic sweep keeps the cap enforced" -> "re-runs the expiry rule on a best-effort cadence", pointing at `_SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS`. (2) `session_store.py:842` section header now states the real model - an UNPROTECTED store becomes expiry-ELIGIBLE at 24 h and is destroyed by the next SUCCESSFUL sweep; protected stores are exempt; an `OSError` records `action="error"` and RETAINS - so what is enforced is the rule and its fail-safe direction, not a deadline. (3) `ui/models.py:223` "never offer recovery of a session past its window" -> the honest form, naming the branch the absolute talked over: with NEITHER timestamp readable, `readable` is empty, both age tests are skipped and the session IS listed regardless of age (deliberate - the sweep owns orphan/expiry decisions). (4) The clinician-facing one, `ui/recovery.py:258` `_on_recovery_failed`: it promised "remains recoverable within its 24-hour window" for EVERY failure class, including a corrupt store or unusable key where a retry cannot succeed. Now states only what the failure establishes - nothing was deleted by the attempt, retrying may or may not succeed depending on the error, discard remains available.
- /fix date: 2026-08-18
- /fix applied by: live Claude-family executor (`claude-opus-5`), `/peer-loop` round 47 fix leg

#### PR-LOW-002: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_config.py:8-13,788-791`; `session_store.py:742-746`; `ui/models.py:658-662` - code still treats user-authored config as structurally non-clinical
- Classification: Same-family (rounds 40-41 config-classification correction)
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: the security docs were narrowed after verifying that `NoteConfig` validates shape, not semantics, but the code still says config is categorically "NOT patient data" and uses that assumption to call detailed validation values safe. A clinician override can contain patient data in any accepted string, and a Pydantic validation error can reproduce rejected input. There is no current exception-logging call site, so this is LOW, but the comments explicitly invite future code to treat a content-bearing exception as safe to display **or log**. The module docstring is independently false when it says this module touches no clinical artifact: `build_note_request` consumes a `TranscriptDocument` and assembles a clinical `NoteRequest`.
- Current behaviour: `_parse_config_blob` includes full validation detail "because config is ... not clinical content"; `_canonical_note` contrasts its terse error with that assumption and says the resulting string is safe anywhere; `config_report_lines` calls user-authored config metadata non-clinical without naming the policy limitation. Structural validation provides none of those semantic guarantees.
- Desired behaviour: describe config as **intended** clinician-authored non-patient boilerplate and state that the loader enforces structure only. Correct the module boundary description to acknowledge the transcript-to-request constructor. If detailed config validation values remain in the local UI, document that narrow destination; do not call them universally safe to log. Apply the same qualification to the report helper's user-authored fields.
- Pattern to follow: `docs/security/threat-model.md`, `data-flow-map.md`, and `retention-schedule.md` after rounds 40-41: config is intended boilerplate, the semantic policy is not machine-enforced, and user-authored content can violate it.
- Pattern siblings: the shipped defaults are in fact non-clinical and need no change; the finding concerns categorical claims about arbitrary clinician overrides and the clinical `TranscriptDocument` consumed at the generation boundary.
- Invariant: unvalidated user-authored strings must never be classified as non-clinical by structure alone, especially when that classification controls whether detailed values are considered log-safe.
- /fix decision: **Applied** as a CLASS sweep. Post-fix grep for `NOT patient data|not clinical content|[Nn]on-clinical` over `src/` returns 9 hits: the 4 corrected, and 5 VERIFIED true BY CONSTRUCTION and retained (`audio_capture` device metadata, `benchmark`'s fixed synthetic script, `logging_setup`'s whitelisted key list, `session.py`'s uuid4 id, `session_store`'s store header).
- /fix notes: (1) `note_config.py:8` - "NOT patient data" is now stated as a POLICY that the validators do not enforce (they constrain shape and character content, never meaning, so nothing may be treated as non-clinical BECAUSE it validated), and the flatly FALSE second half is corrected: no session key, correct, but `build_note_request` in this very module takes a `TranscriptDocument` and assembles the `NoteRequest` carrying the whole transcript - a clinical path, now named as one. (2) `_parse_config_blob` - the included detail is justified by DESTINATION, not safety class: it reproduces rejected input, so it is safe for the LOCAL UI surface, is NOT log-safe, carries no tripwire signature, and no future call site may log it on the strength of "config is not clinical content". (3) `session_store._canonical_note` - the parenthetical that justified the contrast "BECAUSE config is not clinical content" is replaced by the destination distinction; this message remains the log-safe one. (4) `ui/models.config_report_lines` - counts and canonical titles are non-clinical by construction, but the profile `display_name` is user-authored, so the docstring now says display-safe on the local surface rather than non-clinical, and not thereby log-safe.
- /fix date: 2026-08-18
- /fix applied by: live Claude-family executor (`claude-opus-5`), `/peer-loop` round 47 fix leg
- Verification: inspected all config validators and found only shape/content-form constraints, no patient-data classifier; traced `build_note_request(document, ...)` into `_assemble_note_request`; traced current config-load exceptions to local UI display and found no shipping logger call, which bounds severity but does not make the claims true.
- Regression risk: preserve loud, all-or-nothing config failure and actionable local diagnostics while keeping clinical values out of logs. Do not weaken canonical config revalidation or digest binding.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope - code-side sibling of the already-ratified Phase 3A security-document correction.

ROUND-47 RESULT: 3 findings (CRIT 0 / HIGH 0 / MED 1 / LOW 2), all VERIFIED by the live executor and APPLIED; both LOWs swept as classes. Suites after the fix leg: pytest 1243, ruff clean, mypy clean.

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:435-449,636-691` - punctuation and modality loss manufactures blocking negation/laterality contradictions
- Classification: Pre-existing
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: Check 2 emits `error`-severity `contradiction`, which blocks `write_note`, copy, and Complete. Its stated contract and global property 2 require zero false positives. The parser currently erases clause boundaries and then treats questions and negated anatomy mentions as positive laterality facts, so ordinary consistent clinical statements can be hard-blocked.
- Current behaviour: `_tokens_from_text` and `_tokens_from_words` reduce input to `content_tokens`, retaining neither punctuation boundaries nor token-level modality. `_claims_from_tokens` then (a) binds every direct `left/right + anatomy` pair without consulting `interrogative` or nearby negation, and (b) lets any negation bind a symptom one or two flattened tokens later. Thus `No fever; pain persists` becomes `no, fever, pain, persists`, and `no` falsely negates `pain`; an authored `Pain persists` then conflicts. Likewise `No pain in the left knee` still emits a positive `laterality:knee=left`, and a question such as `Is it the left knee?` does too; either can falsely block a consistent right-knee assertion.
- Desired behaviour: an error-grade laterality or negation claim must be mechanically established inside one clause and in asserted polarity. Preserve enough boundary/modality information to prevent a negation crossing punctuation, and suppress laterality extracted from questions or negated/non-asserted anatomy clauses. If the parser cannot establish that structure, fail toward silence/review rather than blocking.
- Pattern to follow: the dose branch's closed positive grammar and explicit fail-toward-review/silence contract in the same module. Do not add an open-ended exception/deny list for punctuation phrases.
- Pattern siblings: apply the correction to both clinician-authored (`_tokens_from_text`) and quoted-transcript (`_tokens_from_words`) paths, and cover both comparison populations (authored/authored and authored/quoted). Sweep interrogative laterality, negated laterality, and negation across sentence/semicolon/clause boundaries as one class.
- Invariant: Check 2 must never emit an error-grade contradiction unless both compared values are positive, same-anchor claims whose incompatible states are established by the retained structure.
- Verification: static token-level trace through `content_tokens` -> `_claims_from_tokens` -> `_consolidated` -> both loops in `contradiction_warnings`; no executable probe was run. Existing true-positive pins cover plain left-vs-right and affirmed-vs-negated symptoms, but no scoped-boundary/modality guard is present in the reviewed implementation.
- Regression risk: preserve the existing true positives (`left knee` vs `right knee`; `pain` vs `no pain`) and low-confidence grading. A fix that globally suppresses every assertion containing a negation would avoid false blocks but widen silence unnecessarily; clause-local structure is the class-level repair.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope - shipping Check 2 correctness and the plan's explicit no-false-positive property.
- /fix decision: **Applied**
- /fix notes: CONFIRMED empirically before fixing - `No pain in the left knee` yielded a positive `laterality:knee=left`, `Is it the left knee?` did too, and `No fever; pain persists` marked `pain` NEGATED across the semicolon; each can hard-block a consistent note. FIX: `_Token` gained a COUNTED `clause` field (never an injected sentinel, so the dose grammar is untouched), incremented on a raw chunk/word ending in `_TERMINAL_PUNCT` - imported from `note_config`, the single shared definition, so the config authoring guard and this parser cannot disagree about what ends a claim. A trailing terminator increments past the last token and changes nothing, which is what keeps the pinned single-clause error cases error-grade. Laterality is now emitted only when the assertion is not interrogative, the pair does not straddle a clause, and the clause carries no negation; negation binds only within the window AND the same clause. Every check only ever DROPS a claim, so no new contradiction can arise from this change (the affirmed branch's whole-assertion rule was deliberately left alone rather than widened to clause scope, which would have ADDED positive claims). HONEST BOUND recorded on the new test: suppression is only as good as `is_interrogative`, which needs a '?' or an auxiliary + PRONOUN subject - "Is the pain in your left knee" (no '?') is still not recognised. Widening it was deliberately NOT done: round 1 MED-001 narrowed `_SUBJECT_TOKENS` on purpose and the function also drives provider routing. Three new tests; all pre-existing true positives (left-vs-right, affirmed-vs-negated, the low-confidence grading) still pass.
- /fix date: 2026-08-18
- /fix applied by: live Claude-family executor (`claude-opus-5`), `/peer-loop` round 48 fix leg

#### PR-MED-002: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:775-805` - the exclusive dose grammar hard-blocks bare product-strength wording without current-regimen evidence
- Classification: Pre-existing
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: the comments and tests define the hard-error population positively: a mechanically-established current regimen fact with one exclusive strength slot. `_exclusive_context` only proves that no token is outside its vocabulary; it does not prove that any administration/current-state token exists. Ambiguous product-strength shorthand can therefore become a blocking contradiction instead of the deliberately acknowledgeable `dose_mismatch` review.
- Current behaviour: `Paracetamol 250 mg` and `Paracetamol 500 mg` each collapse to the identical context `("paracetamol", "<dose>")`. Every token passes `_exclusive_context` because medication names and `<dose>` are allowed, so `_same_state` returns true and the differing values emit `contradiction`/error. The wording establishes a medication-strength relation, but not whether it is a current dose, a product label, stock, or two simultaneously held strengths. This contradicts the module's own statement that product strength and anything short of a provably exclusive regimen demote to review.
- Desired behaviour: require positive, mechanically checkable current-administration/state evidence in addition to token identity and the closed vocabulary before returning true from `_same_state`. Bare medication-strength wording and any other context without that positive witness must grade `dose_mismatch`; explicit identical-context regimen facts such as `I take paracetamol <dose> twice daily` or `Paracetamol <dose> advised` must remain blocking.
- Pattern to follow: the existing inventory/product-strength tests and the allow-list direction established in rounds 23-24: ambiguity demotes to review, never to an error. Express the positive witness as a closed grammar, not a deny list of stock phrases.
- Pattern siblings: both authored/authored and authored/quoted comparison loops call `_same_state`; attached and separated units produce the same collapsed context and need the same guard. Sweep the whole `_EXCLUSIVE_DOSE_CONTEXT` vocabulary for tokens that are merely connectors/timing nouns versus tokens or forms that actually establish administration/current state.
- Invariant: a differing dose may hard-block only when the checker positively establishes the same medication, unit, and exclusive clinical state; absence of disqualifying words is not proof of that state.
- Verification: static trace through `_strength_atoms`, `_dose_binding`, `_dose_claims`, `_exclusive_context`, `_same_state`, and both comparison loops. The bare context contains only the medication plus placeholder, yet `all(...)` returns true; no executable probe was run.
- Regression risk: preserve every existing explicit same-state error pin and low-confidence downgrade. Do not make the positive witness so broad that connector-only shorthand recreates the same false-block class under another spelling.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope - shipping dose severity, directly inside the requested Check 2 review.
- /fix decision: **Applied**
- /fix notes: CONFIRMED empirically - `Paracetamol 250 mg` and `Paracetamol 500 mg` both collapse to the context `('paracetamol', '<dose>')`, every token is allowed, so `_same_state` returned True and the pair hard-blocked, contradicting this module's own contract that product strength demotes to review. FIX: `_exclusive_context` now has TWO conditions - the existing NEGATIVE test (no token outside the closed vocabulary) plus a new POSITIVE one, `_ADMINISTRATION_WITNESS`, requiring at least one token that actually asserts administration or pins a schedule. SIZED AGAINST THE EXISTING PINS rather than invented: `paracetamol 500 mg in the morning` and `paracetamol 1000mg at night` are pinned ERROR-grade, so a witness set of administration verbs alone would have broken them - timing/frequency words are therefore witnesses, while pure structure (`i`, `each`, `every`, `in`, `the`, `at`, `a`, `per`, `dose`, `of`), medication names and `<dose>` are not. Verified: bare product strength -> review; `advised`, `in the morning`, `at night` pairs -> still error. Two new tests, one of them pinning that the witness set is a strict SUBSET of `_EXCLUSIVE_DOSE_CONTEXT` (a token outside it could never fire and would read as coverage while being dead).
- /fix date: 2026-08-18
- /fix applied by: live Claude-family executor (`claude-opus-5`), `/peer-loop` round 48 fix leg

#### PR-LOW-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/recovery.py:251-268`; `transcription.py:797-830` - the Round-47 recovery copy falsely says a failed attempt deleted nothing
- Classification: Fix-induced (Round 47 PR-LOW-001)
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: the replacement correctly stopped promising that every failed recovery remains recoverable, but it introduced a different unconditional assurance. A recovery can fail after deleting a saved encrypted draft, so the clinician-facing message can deny a real state change and obscure why the note needs regenerating.
- Current behaviour: `_on_recovery_failed` always says `Nothing was deleted by this attempt.` Recovery calls `recover_session_transcription` -> `transcribe_session` -> `write_transcript`. `write_transcript` deliberately unlinks stale `note.enc` before the atomic transcript write; its Round-45-corrected contract explicitly records that if unlink succeeds and the following write fails (or the process dies), the old transcript remains but the ratified note is gone. That exception reaches this failure handler.
- Desired behaviour: state only the invariant the handler actually knows: the attempt did not deliberately discard session custody/key material. Keep the honest retry/discard guidance, and do not claim that no artifact was deleted; optionally explain that a prior draft may need regeneration if failure occurred during the final rewrite.
- Pattern to follow: `write_transcript`'s precise unlink-then-write residual at lines 808-820 and Round 47's own intended wording in the adjacent comment (`this path discarded no custody deliberately`).
- Pattern siblings: the corrected expiry/cadence wording at `app.py`, `session_store.py`, and `ui/models.py` is accurate and should remain unchanged. Sweep only recovery-result messages that make artifact-preservation claims after `transcribe_session`.
- Invariant: recovery failure copy must not promise preservation of an artifact that the attempted recovery is allowed to remove before a later operation fails.
- Verification: static call trace from the recovery task's failure signal through `_on_recovery_failed`, with the only final transcript write path inspected. The unlink occurs before the operation that can raise `StoreWriteError`; no executable fault injection was run.
- Regression risk: do not regress to Round 47's old promise of recoverability within a hard 24-hour window, and do not imply that key custody was destroyed. Preserve clear retry and explicit Discard options.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope - direct post-fix audit of the operator-named Round-47 change.
- /fix decision: **Applied**
- /fix notes: CONFIRMED - round 47 replaced one unconditional assurance with another, and this one is false: recovery reaches `write_transcript`, which unlinks a stale `note.enc` BEFORE the atomic transcript write, so a failure in that write arrives at this handler with a saved draft already gone - the exact window round 45 LOW-003 documented one round earlier. The message now states only what holds on EVERY path (no session key was discarded, i.e. no cryptographic deletion), names the note as the one artifact a failed rewrite can have removed, and keeps the honest retry/discard guidance without promising either outcome.
- /fix date: 2026-08-18
- /fix applied by: live Claude-family executor (`claude-opus-5`), `/peer-loop` round 48 fix leg

#### PR-LOW-002: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_config.py:796-813`; `ui/transcript.py:248-257,438-445` - actionable config validation detail is hidden on the normal load-failure path
- Classification: Same-family (Round 47 PR-LOW-002, destination-bounded config diagnostics)
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: `_parse_config_blob` deliberately includes Pydantic detail because a clinician editing plaintext config must be told what to repair. On the normal initial-load failure, the shipping UI discards the filename, field path, and validation reason, leaving only the exception class and disabling generation. The diagnostic is destination-bounded in principle but does not reach its intended destination when it is most needed.
- Current behaviour: `_parse_config_blob` says its detailed, potentially patient-bearing message is safe for the local UI surface `it is shown on`. `TranscriptScreen.show_document` catches every `NoteConfigError` and renders only `type(exc).__name__`; the user sees `NoteConfigInvalidError` (or `NoteConfigUnreadableError`) with no actionable repair detail. Only the later worker-reload failure path includes `str(exc)`, and that path is unreachable when the initial load already failed and Generate was disabled.
- Desired behaviour: surface an actionable local-only config error on initial load (at minimum filename/location plus field/reason), while preserving the Round-47 rule that the content is not log-safe. Because rejected input is user-authored and may contain markup-like text, render it through an explicitly plain-text UI path rather than relying on `QLabel` AutoText; alternatively make `_parse_config_blob` produce a bounded plain diagnostic that names the repair without reproducing arbitrary input.
- Pattern to follow: the generation task's `_on_compose_failed` path already surfaces `str(exc)` locally, while the note/transcript bodies use `setPlainText` for content-bearing strings. Keep destination and rendering mode explicit.
- Pattern siblings: cover malformed and unreadable initial config for all three files, resolved-config validation failures, and the worker reload path. No sibling may log the detailed exception or call it structurally non-clinical.
- Invariant: a loud, total config failure must tell the clinician what local file/field to repair without sending user-authored values to logs or interpreting them as rich text.
- Verification: traced `_read_config_blob` / `_parse_config_blob` / `load_note_config` into both shipping consumers: `show_document` drops `str(exc)` and disables Generate; `build_note_generator` can expose it only after a successful initial load. `message_label` has no explicit `PlainText` format. No executable UI probe was run.
- Regression risk: displaying raw Pydantic text in the existing AutoText `QLabel` would trade missing diagnostics for markup interpretation. Preserve all-or-nothing load, no fallback to defaults, and no logging.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope - direct completeness audit of the Round-47 destination-bounded fix and the requested config/transcript UI files.
- /fix decision: **Applied**
- /fix notes: CONFIRMED - `_populate_generation_controls` rendered only `type(exc).__name__`, discarding the filename and field path exactly when Generate is about to be disabled, so round 47's "safe for the LOCAL UI surface it is shown on" described a surface it never reached. FIX, both halves the peer asked for: (a) the initial-load handler now surfaces the full loader message; (b) the rendering-mode hazard is closed as a CLASS, not at the one site - ALL FOUR `message_label` QLabels (`note`, `recovery`, `session_screen`, `transcript`) are now explicitly `Qt.TextFormat.PlainText`, since each renders exception text that can reproduce user-authored config or note content and AutoText would interpret markup-like input as rich text. That is the discipline `ui/note.py` already applied to the proposal excerpt label, now applied everywhere it belongs. `session_screen.py` needed a `Qt` import it did not have. No logging was added on any path.
- /fix date: 2026-08-18
- /fix applied by: live Claude-family executor (`claude-opus-5`), `/peer-loop` round 48 fix leg

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:708-734` - clause-wide negation suppresses an explicit positive laterality after an unrelated negation
- Classification: Fix-induced (Round 48 PR-MED-001)
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: this is the unsafe direction of Round 48's laterality repair. A confirmed left/right assertion can now disagree with an explicit, high-confidence transcript side and reach `write_note` without the Check-2 error the plan's laterality-flip class requires. Per-assertion confirmation remains a compensating control, but it is not a substitute for the checker class the phase says it implements.
- Current behaviour: `_claims_from_tokens` builds `negated_clauses` from every negation token and drops every laterality+anatomy pair whose numeric clause appears in that set. For the exact transcript assertion `No fever but right knee pain.` all tokens are clause 0, so `no` suppresses the explicit `right knee` pair even though the adversative positive subclause is unambiguous. Against a confirmed `Left knee strapped.`, the authored side has `laterality:knee=left`, the quoted side has no laterality claim, their keys never intersect, and no `contradiction` is emitted. Before Round 48, this true positive was present.
- Desired behaviour: suppress a laterality pair only when negation mechanically scopes to that pair (or its local subclause), not merely because a negation occurs somewhere in the same punctuation clause. Preserve the fixes for `No pain in the left knee.`, interrogatives, pair-straddling, and `No fever; pain persists.`; do not widen the deliberately whole-assertion rule for the separate affirmed-symptom branch. An explicit positive subclause after a mechanically recognised contrast boundary must still yield its laterality claim.
- Pattern to follow: the symptom branch already combines a structural boundary with a bounded relation instead of treating clause membership alone as scope. Give laterality an equally local polarity rule; keep the existing counted clause and interrogative gates as independent guards.
- Pattern siblings: cover quoted-vs-authored and authored-vs-authored comparisons; `no`/`not`/`denies`; adversatives such as `but` (and any other mechanically supported contrast token); negation before an actually negated laterality phrase; and terminal-punctuation separation. Assert high-confidence mismatch severity and quoted evidence coordinates for the retained positive pair.
- Invariant: an unrelated negative proposition in the same punctuation clause must not erase a mechanically explicit positive laterality+anatomy claim, while a negation that actually governs that claim must not manufacture a positive side.
- Verification: static token/claim trace through `_tokens_from_words` -> `_claims_from_tokens` -> `_consolidated` -> `contradiction_warnings`; no executable probe or test suite was run.
- Regression risk: a broad relaxation would reintroduce Round 48's false-positive block for `No pain in the left knee.`. Keep `source_coords` tied to the laterality and anatomy words, preserve word-index alignment, and do not change dose or affirmed-symptom semantics as collateral.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope - direct post-fix audit of the operator-named Round-48 checker change and its required true-positive side.
- /fix decision: **Applied**
- /fix notes: CONFIRMED empirically - `No fever but right knee pain.` produced NO laterality claim at all, so a real laterality-flip against a confirmed `Left knee strapped.` was no longer caught. This is round 48's over-correction in the UNSAFE direction, exactly as filed. FIX: a closed `_CONTRAST_TOKENS` allow-list (`but`, `however`, `though`, `although`, `whereas`, `yet`, `except`) opens a new SUBCLAUSE on the same counted `clause` field, so a negation before an adversative no longer reaches the assertion after it. Chosen over a backward window because the round-48 case it must keep fixing (`No pain in the left knee.`) puts the negation FOUR tokens before the laterality token - a window wide enough to catch it would also swallow the contrast case. Allow-list direction preserved: an unlisted contrast word leaves the clause undivided, i.e. round-48 behaviour, which fails toward silence not toward a false block. The affirmed-symptom branch's whole-assertion rule was left alone as the peer required. Verified: the true positive is restored AND all three round-48 fixes plus every earlier true positive still hold.
- /fix date: 2026-08-18
- /fix applied by: live Claude-family executor (`claude-opus-5`), `/peer-loop` round 49 fix leg

#### PR-MED-002: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:553-687,851-906` - dose atoms, bindings, and error-grade context ignore clause boundaries
- Classification: Same-family (Round 48 PR-MED-001/002; the clause/witness class was closed only for laterality and symptom negation)
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: Check 2 promises to hard-block a dose mismatch only when medication, unit, and one exclusive current-regimen state are mechanically established. The parser now knows where terminal punctuation falls, but every dose stage discards that fact. It can therefore construct a medication-strength relation across sentences or borrow an administration witness from an unrelated clause, restoring the same false-positive blocking direction Round 48 intended to remove.
- Current behaviour: `_strength_atoms` can join a number and separated unit across clauses; `_connector_walk` and all three `_dose_binding` relations can cross clauses; `_dose_claims` collapses a context from the entire assertion; and `_exclusive_context` accepts a witness anywhere in that assertion. For example, an exact quoted `Paracetamol. 500 mg advised.` binds the clause-1 strength to clause-0 `paracetamol`, collapses to the same `("paracetamol", "<dose>", "advised")` context as confirmed `Paracetamol 250 mg advised.`, finds the `advised` witness, and emits a blocking `contradiction`. Terminal punctuation means the relation is not mechanically established by the closed local grammar. The same whole-assertion context lets a bare product-strength clause inherit `continue`, a schedule token, or another witness from a different clause.
- Desired behaviour: a separated strength atom must stay within one clause; every medication, connector/relation token, and atom used by a dose binding must share that clause; and the collapsed error-grade context plus `_ADMINISTRATION_WITNESS` search must be restricted to the bound dose's clause. Cross-clause forms should fail toward review/silence under the existing contract, while the pinned single-clause regimen facts remain error-grade.
- Pattern to follow: Round 48 made clause identity explicit on `_Token`; consume that identity at each dose relation boundary instead of reconstructing punctuation or injecting sentinel tokens. Continue deriving confidence and coordinates from the exact medication/relationship/atom tokens that establish a valid same-clause binding.
- Pattern siblings: exercise attached and separated units; R1 medication-first, R2 `<atom> of <medication>`, and R3 `dose of`; connectors at a clause edge; an unrelated later-clause administration witness; authored-vs-authored and quoted-vs-authored pairs; and all terminal characters in the shared `_TERMINAL_PUNCT` set. Retain the existing single-clause morning/night/advised/take fixtures and low-confidence relation-word grading.
- Invariant: no dose claim or error-grade same-state witness may depend on tokens from more than one clause; a valid single-clause binding must keep its current source-word indices and confidence evidence.
- Verification: static data-flow trace through `_strength_atoms`, `_connector_walk`, `_dose_binding`, `_dose_claims`, `_exclusive_context`, `_same_state`, and both comparison loops in `contradiction_warnings`; no executable probe or test suite was run.
- Regression risk: over-scoping could silence the true single-clause dose conflicts hardened in rounds 21-24 or corrupt evidence coordinates. Preserve attached/separated equivalence, R1/R2/R3 precedence and ambiguity handling, unit-in-anchor behaviour, low-confidence grading, and the Round-48 bare-product review case.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope - direct completeness audit of the operator-named clause counter and administration-witness changes in the shipping checker.
- /fix decision: **Applied**
- /fix notes: CONFIRMED empirically - `Paracetamol. 500 mg advised.` bound the clause-1 strength to the clause-0 medication and hard-blocked against `Paracetamol 250 mg advised.`, and a bare product-strength clause borrowed a witness from a later sentence. Round 48 gave the parser clause identity and consumed it in only two of five places; this closes the remaining three. FIX, at each dose stage: `_strength_atoms` requires a separated unit to share its number's clause; `_connector_walk` takes the clause and requires every walked connector AND the medication to sit in it; all three `_dose_binding` relations (R1/R2/R3) require their `of`/`dose`/medication tokens to share the atom's clause; and `_dose_claims` collapses the context from the atom's clause ONLY, which also scopes `_exclusive_context`'s witness search. Verified: cross-clause binding now yields NO claim, the borrowed-witness pair demotes to `dose_mismatch` review, and all five pinned single-clause error cases (`advised`, `in the morning`, `at night`, `I take ... twice daily`, `500 mg of paracetamol advised`) plus round 48's bare-product review case are unchanged. R1/R2/R3 precedence, ambiguity handling, unit-in-anchor, and evidence coordinates/confidence untouched.
- /fix date: 2026-08-18
- /fix applied by: live Claude-family executor (`claude-opus-5`), `/peer-loop` round 49 fix leg
