#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:476-503,713-718,751-773,922-929` - the contrast splitter is neither dose-neutral nor polarity-safe
- Classification: Fix-induced (Round 49 PR-MED-001/002)
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: the new subclause signal participates in every dose stage and in laterality polarity. Its current construction can demote a mechanically explicit current-regimen conflict to review, or manufacture a positive laterality claim out of negated wording. The latter can emit an error-grade `contradiction` and hard-block write, copy, and Complete.
- Current behaviour: `_tokens_from_text` and `_tokens_from_words` increment `clause` BEFORE appending every token in `_CONTRAST_TOKENS`. The boundary word therefore belongs to the new clause. In `No ibuprofen, but I take paracetamol 250 mg daily.`, the bound dose context becomes `("but", "i", "take", "paracetamol", "<dose>", "daily")`; `but` is outside `_EXCLUSIVE_DOSE_CONTEXT`, so an otherwise identical confirmed 500 mg regimen grades only `dose_mismatch`. More seriously, the allow-list is lexical rather than grammatical: temporal `yet` in `No evidence yet of left knee pain.` unconditionally opens a new clause, moves `left knee` away from `no`, and emits a positive left-knee claim that can hard-block a consistent right-knee assertion. The inverse direction is also incomplete: preposed `Although no fever, right knee pain persists.` puts the subordinate negation and the positive main-clause laterality in the same newly opened clause, so the real right-knee claim is suppressed.
- Desired behaviour: recognise only mechanically established contrast boundaries, in a direction that preserves the polarity of each side, and keep the boundary marker itself out of the clinical clause context used by `_same_state`. Ambiguous/polysemous uses must fail toward silence/review. Preserve the named Round-49 true positive (`No fever but right knee pain.`) without turning temporal `yet` into a negation escape or poisoning a valid post-contrast regimen with the word `but`.
- Pattern to follow: the dose parser's R1/R2/R3 relations use positional closed grammars, not token membership alone. Apply the same structural discipline to contrast: token identity plus a mechanically bounded position/direction, with the boundary represented as metadata rather than a semantic context token.
- Pattern siblings: both authored/authored and authored/quoted loops; all seven `_CONTRAST_TOKENS`; postposed `but/however/yet`, preposed `although/though/whereas`, temporal `yet`, exception `except`, laterality and dose contexts, and source-coordinate preservation for transcript evidence.
- Invariant: a contrast boundary may limit a negation's scope, but the boundary word itself cannot establish or disqualify a dose state, and a non-adversative use cannot turn a negated anatomy mention into a positive laterality claim.
- Verification: add pins for a post-contrast explicit regimen conflict (must remain error-grade), temporal `yet` under negation (must not emit positive laterality), and a preposed adversative with a positive main clause (must retain the laterality true positive), through both token sources and both comparison loops. Re-run every Round-48/49 negation/laterality/dose pin and assert unchanged transcript coordinates.
- Regression risk: moving or omitting the adversative carelessly could reintroduce `No fever but right knee pain.` silence, shift a bounded negation window, or let a dose context borrow tokens across the boundary. Keep word indices attached to the original `TranscriptWord` even if a boundary token is excluded from semantic context.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope - direct audit of the unaudited Round-49 contrast/clause fix and the Check-2 contract the operator named.
- /fix decision: **Applied**
- /fix notes: All three sub-claims CONFIRMED empirically. (a) `No ibuprofen, but I take paracetamol 250 mg daily.` collapsed to a context CONTAINING `but`, which is outside `_EXCLUSIVE_DOSE_CONTEXT`, so a genuine current-regimen conflict graded review. FIXED by incrementing the clause AFTER appending the contrast token: the adversative now CLOSES the negated subclause instead of opening the new one, so the boundary marker can neither establish nor disqualify a clinical state. (b) `No evidence yet of left knee pain.` emitted a positive `laterality:knee=left`. FIXED by narrowing `_CONTRAST_TOKENS` to `{but, however}` - a contrast boundary only ever LIMITS a negation's scope, i.e. only ever ADDS positive claims, so a word earns a place only if its contrastive reading is unambiguous; `yet` is overwhelmingly temporal. (c) The preposed-subordinator direction (`Although no fever, right knee pain persists.`) is the reason `though`/`although`/`whereas`/`except` were dropped rather than kept: opening a clause AT the subordinator puts the subordinate negation and the main clause's positive claim on the SAME side, achieving nothing. Recognising preposed clauses needs grammar this module does not have, so that case now fails toward SILENCE (a missed true positive, never a false block) and is recorded as a NAMED RESIDUE in the `_CONTRAST_TOKENS` comment rather than left implied. Verified: the round-49 true positive `No fever but right knee pain.` survives, and the `but` regimen conflict now blocks correctly.
- /fix date: 2026-08-18
- /fix applied by: live Claude-family executor (`claude-opus-5`), `/peer-loop` round 50 fix leg

#### PR-MED-002: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:734-810,830-870` - recognised questions still produce negated-symptom and dose claims that can hard-block
- Classification: Same-family (Round 48 PR-MED-001, incomplete modality repair)
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: Check 2 may compare only asserted incompatible facts. A question is not an assertion, yet two of the three claim branches still parse recognised questions and can emit an error-grade contradiction. This is the exact false-positive blocking direction Round 48's modality fix was intended to close.
- Current behaviour: `_structured_authored` and `_structured_quoted` correctly pass `is_interrogative(...)` into `_claims_from_tokens`, and laterality plus affirmed symptoms honour it. `_dose_claims` is nevertheless appended unconditionally, and the negated-symptom branch emits whenever a nearby negation exists without checking `interrogative`. Thus confirmed `No numbness?` versus `Numbness persists.` produces negated-vs-affirmed `contradiction`; likewise `On paracetamol 250 mg?` versus `On paracetamol 500 mg.` produces identical exclusive contexts, with `on` as an administration witness, and hard-blocks. Both examples end in `?`, so this finding does not widen or challenge the deliberately recorded `is_interrogative` recognition bound.
- Desired behaviour: once the existing heuristic has positively recognised an assertion as interrogative, no laterality, dose, affirmed-symptom, or negated-symptom claim from it may enter the blocking comparison population. Keep the heuristic itself unchanged.
- Pattern to follow: the current laterality guard at `_claims_from_tokens` and the affirmed-symptom `not interrogative` gate already enforce the right direction. Make that modality gate cover the whole structured-claim extraction boundary instead of selected branches.
- Pattern siblings: dose and negated-symptom branches; authored/authored and authored/quoted comparisons; trailing-`?` and auxiliary-plus-pronoun questions; high- and low-confidence transcript words. The recorded no-`?`, non-pronoun-subject heuristic bound remains out of scope.
- Invariant: no error-grade contradiction can rely on a claim extracted from text that `is_interrogative` already classifies as a question.
- Verification: pin the two recognised-question examples above in both comparison populations, assert zero `contradiction` errors, and retain the existing declarative left/right, dose, and affirmed-vs-negated true positives without changing `note.is_interrogative` or provider routing.
- Regression risk: changing the shared question heuristic would reopen Round 1's imperative-routing regression. Keep the repair local to Check 2's consumption of the already-computed boolean.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope - direct sibling audit of Round 48's Check-2 modality repair.
- /fix decision: **Applied**
- /fix notes: CONFIRMED empirically - `No numbness?` (trailing `?`, so already recognised by the heuristic) produced a `negation` claim, and `On paracetamol 250 mg?` vs `On paracetamol 500 mg.` returned `_same_state` True, i.e. a hard block from a QUESTION. Round 48 had honoured `interrogative` in the laterality and affirmed-symptom branches only, while `_dose_claims` ran unconditionally and the negated-symptom branch never consulted it. FIXED STRUCTURALLY at the single entry: `_claims_from_tokens` returns no claims at all for a recognised question, so the rule cannot be forgotten by a future branch - the same one-exit discipline `MockNoteModelProvider`'s fingerprint check uses. This does not touch the recorded `is_interrogative` RECOGNITION bound; it only ensures what the heuristic does recognise is honoured everywhere.
- /fix date: 2026-08-18
- /fix applied by: live Claude-family executor (`claude-opus-5`), `/peer-loop` round 50 fix leg

#### PR-MED-003: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:687-729,877-929` - the administration witness that upgrades a dose to blocking is absent from confidence and coordinate evidence
- Classification: Same-family (Round 48 PR-MED-002, incomplete positive-witness evidence)
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: `_ADMINISTRATION_WITNESS` is now the positive fact that distinguishes a blocking current regimen from ambiguous product strength. If that decisive transcript word is low-confidence, the checker still issues an unacknowledgeable error instead of the required review-grade warning, and the warning points at coordinates that omit the evidence on which the severity upgrade depended.
- Current behaviour: `_dose_claims` builds `involved` from only the medication, binding connectors/relation words, and strength atom before calling `_claim_evidence`. It later builds the whole clause context, and `_exclusive_context` searches that context for a witness, but the witness token never joins `min_probability` or `source_coords`. For quoted `paracetamol 250 mg advised` with only `advised` below `UNCERTAINTY_THRESHOLD`, compared with confirmed `paracetamol 500 mg advised`, the contexts match and `advised` upgrades the pair to exclusive current-state identity, while the recorded probability remains the high minimum of words 0-2. The result is `contradiction`/error with coordinates 0-2 rather than `contradiction_low_confidence`/review with evidence reaching word 3.
- Desired behaviour: every transcript token whose identity is necessary to cross from `dose_mismatch` review into error-grade same-state identity must participate in the claim's confidence and coordinates. At minimum this includes the actual administration/schedule witness selected from the bound atom's clause; if context-token identity is also relied on as proof, treat that evidence consistently.
- Pattern to follow: Round 24's `_DoseBinding.evidence` exists specifically so low-confidence `of`, `dose`, and connector words cannot establish a hard error invisibly. Carry the Round-48 positive witness through the same evidence path rather than re-discovering it only as a string in the collapsed context.
- Pattern siblings: witnesses before and after the atom; R1/R2/R3; attached and separated units; every `_ADMINISTRATION_WITNESS` member; quoted/authored and authored/authored comparisons (the latter has no acoustic grading and should remain error-grade when otherwise exclusive).
- Invariant: no transcript-side token required to establish an error-grade dose state is excluded from `min_probability` or from the warning coordinates.
- Verification: add a low-confidence-witness twin for an existing high-confidence `advised`/schedule error pin and require `contradiction_low_confidence`, review severity, and coordinates spanning the witness. Retain high-confidence error cases, low-confidence medication/unit/relation grading, and clause-scoped coordinate alignment.
- Regression risk: indiscriminately including an entire long clause could make coordinates noisy and demote on irrelevant filler. Track the bounded tokens that mechanically establish exclusivity, preserving the closed grammar and existing R1/R2/R3 precedence.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope - direct audit of the Round-48 `_ADMINISTRATION_WITNESS` change named by the operator.
- /fix decision: **Applied**
- /fix notes: CONFIRMED - the witness is the token that decides whether a dose pair may hard-block at all, yet `involved` held only the medication, the binding relation tokens and the atom, so the witness reached neither `min_probability` nor `source_coords`. FIXED by adding the atom's-clause witnesses to `involved` (a deliberate superset of 'the selected witness', since `_exclusive_context` uses `any(...)` and no single witness is distinguished - the superset can only LOWER min_probability, i.e. demote toward review, the safe direction). Verified with a probe: quoted `paracetamol 250 mg advised` with `advised` at p=0.30 now yields `contradiction_low_confidence`/review with coordinates reaching word 3, where it previously yielded `contradiction`/error with coordinates 0-2. TWO EXISTING PINS UPDATED, not weakened: `test_a_low_confidence_separated_unit_grades_to_review` (span 0-2 -> 0-5) and `test_a_low_confidence_inverse_relation_word_grades_to_review` (0-3 -> 0-4). Each still asserts its original point - that the span reaches THROUGH the separated unit / through R2's `of` - and each now additionally covers the witness; the reason is recorded inline in both tests.
- /fix date: 2026-08-18
- /fix applied by: live Claude-family executor (`claude-opus-5`), `/peer-loop` round 50 fix leg

#### PR-MED-004: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:481-521,785-805`; `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_config.py:427-479` - a terminal followed by a closing wrapper is invisible to the clause counter
- Classification: Same-family (Round 48 PR-MED-001, incomplete clause-ending class)
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: `content_tokens` strips surrounding punctuation, so a visually explicit sentence end followed by a closing quote or bracket becomes indistinguishable from no boundary. A nearby negation can then cross that end and manufacture an error-grade contradiction; dose atoms and relations can likewise bind across it.
- Current behaviour: `_ends_clause` checks only the final non-whitespace character of each raw chunk/word. In the exact quoted text `"No fever." Pain persists.`, the raw word `fever."` ends in a quote, not a member of `_TERMINAL_PUNCT`, while tokenisation still yields `no`, `fever`, `pain`, `persists`. `no` and `pain` remain two token positions apart in one counted clause, so the quoted assertion produces `pain=negated`; a confirmed `Pain persists.` produces `pain=affirmed`, and Check 2 hard-blocks a consistent note. The same shape exists for a terminal followed by a closing parenthesis/bracket and in both `_tokens_from_text` and `_tokens_from_words`.
- Desired behaviour: recognise a shared terminal punctuation character when it is followed only by a closed set of closing wrappers/end punctuation, without treating decimal points, times/ratios, abbreviation-chain interior dots, or arbitrary embedded punctuation as clause ends. Keep `_TERMINAL_PUNCT` as the one terminal-character definition; make `_ends_clause` classify the suffix shape rather than only its last character.
- Pattern to follow: `_is_atomic_shape` already distinguishes terminal positions from decimal/time and abbreviation-chain dots by inspecting surrounding/suffix shape. The transcript checker needs its own bounded closing-wrapper suffix rule because transcript words are not constrained by the config authoring shape, but it should continue consuming the shared `_TERMINAL_PUNCT` set.
- Pattern siblings: straight/curly quotes, parentheses and square brackets; all ASCII/CJK terminal members; both token sources; negation, laterality, separated-unit atoms, R1/R2/R3, and source-coordinate evidence. Pin `1.5`, `14:30`, and `q.i.d.`/abbreviation cases against accidental over-splitting.
- Invariant: a mechanically visible clause terminator cannot be erased merely because a closing wrapper follows it, and non-terminal punctuation inside a lexical/decimal/time/abbreviation token cannot invent a boundary.
- Verification: add an authored and quoted closing-quote boundary false-positive pin, a cross-boundary dose relation pin, and a table over terminal-plus-wrapper suffixes; assert zero error for the consistent cases and unchanged word-index/source-coordinate evidence for valid same-clause conflicts.
- Regression risk: stripping all trailing punctuation wholesale would over-recognise decimals, times, abbreviations, or malformed Whisper chunks. Use a closed suffix grammar and re-run every Round-48/49 clause test plus the matrix's Property-2 clean compositions.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope - direct completeness audit of the Round-48 clause counter and the shared terminal definition explicitly named by the operator.
- /fix decision: **Applied**
- /fix notes: CONFIRMED empirically - `"No fever." Pain persists.` produced `pain=negated`, because `_ends_clause` tested only the chunk's LAST character and `fever."` ends in a quote. FIXED by classifying the trailing SUFFIX SHAPE: strip a closed `_CLOSING_WRAPPERS` set (closing quotes/brackets) and then test against the one shared `_TERMINAL_PUNCT` definition, which is left as the single terminal-character source exactly as the peer required. Only closing wrappers are skipped and only AFTER a real terminator, so a decimal (`1.5`), a time (`14:30`) and an abbreviation chain's interior dots still end in a digit or letter and invent no boundary.
- /fix date: 2026-08-18
- /fix applied by: live Claude-family executor (`claude-opus-5`), `/peer-loop` round 50 fix leg

ROUND-50 RESULT: 4 findings (CRIT 0 / HIGH 0 / MED 4 / LOW 0), all VERIFIED (every mechanism reproduced empirically) and all APPLIED. Suites after the fix leg: pytest 1256 (+5 tests, 2 existing coordinate pins updated with the reason recorded inline), ruff clean, mypy clean.

The NARROW scope was the right call: a single-module deep pass found MORE than either wide round (4 vs 3 and 4 across the whole phase), and every finding was in the module the operator scoped it to. Three of the four were siblings of the round-48/49 fixes rather than fresh ground, which is the same fix-audits-fix pattern rounds 47-49 showed - but note the shape has changed: round 50 found no defect OUTSIDE the Check-2 clause/witness work, and Checks 1, 3 and 4, the pipeline, config, logging and custody surfaces all came back clean under a fresh cross-family reading for the second consecutive round. NOT CONVERGED: round 50 closed with findings, and its own fixes are unaudited.

## Tasks

### Phase 1 — Foundations, types, providers, custody
- [x] 🟩 1.0: Capture both clinics' real Cliniko treatment-note template section lists, and confirm whether they are identical. **Precedes the schema constant** — the canonical set is only defensible as a superset once both real templates have been seen.

  **Template A — captured 2026-08-04** (three groups, six text fields, one checkbox):

  | Group | Field | Type |
  |---|---|---|
  | History | Presenting complaint/patient progress | rich text (HTML) |
  | Examination | Assessment | plain text |
  | Examination | Informed Consent | **checkbox — never written by this app** (see Critical Constraints) |
  | Examination | Diagnosis | plain text |
  | Treatment/Management | Treatment | plain text |
  | Treatment/Management | Response to treatment | plain text |
  | Treatment/Management | Management/Advice | plain text |

  Three findings this capture forces, all to be resolved when the second template arrives:
  - **The canonical schema is missing interval history.** "Presenting complaint/**patient progress**" carries progress-since-last-appointment, which is distinct from presenting complaint (canonical 1) and from response to treatment (canonical 10 — within-session). On a return-visit-heavy caseload this is the highest-volume field in the template, and nothing currently maps to it. Add a canonical section.
  - **The mapping model must express more than `{canonical_key: target_field}`.** Targets carry a group, a content type (HTML vs plain — never emit formatting into a plain field), and in one case a non-text type that is explicitly unmappable.
  - **Six-into-seventeen is heavily many-to-one.** Four canonical sections had no natural target; resolved 2026-08-04 by folding three into existing fields and marking `consent` intentionally unmapped (see `Schema / Data Changes`). The round-2 "Unmapped content" target therefore has no consumer under Template A — keep it, since a future practitioner's mapping will leave genuine gaps, but it is not exercised by this template.

  - **Confirmed 2026-08-04: both clinics use this same template, and Management/Advice is the last field.** So there is exactly ONE mapping profile today. The per-practitioner mapping layer stays — it is the commercial requirement, for future practitioners with different templates — but `template_profile_id` resolves to the sole profile automatically. Do NOT make the clinician choose a profile from a list of one; surface a chooser only when more than one exists.
  - **Done 2026-08-04.** The template is recorded, the canonical set is reconciled (17 sections with stable keys, `progress_since_last_visit` added, listed in `Schema / Data Changes`), every key carries its meaning, ownership and target, and the unmapped-content question is resolved. Carried to Task 3.1: practitioner confirmation of the two non-obvious mapping calls (`outcome_measures` → Assessment; `referrals_investigations` / `follow_up_review` → Management/Advice).
- [x] 🟩 1.1: Note types in `note.py` — `NoteAssertion`, `NoteSpan`, `NoteProposal`, `ConfirmationDecision`, `GeneratedSection`, `NoteWarning`, `GeneratedNote`, `NoteRequest`, `NoteModelProvider` Protocol, and the canonical 17-section constant (listed in `Schema / Data Changes`). Frozen pydantic, `extra="forbid"`, mirroring `TranscriptDocument` (`transcription.py:252-292`). Reuse `SESSION_ID_PATTERN`.
  - **`NoteAssertion` is the unit `GeneratedSection` holds** — not `NoteSpan`. A `transcript` assertion holds exactly one span with one contiguous `source_coords = (segment_index, first_word_index, last_word_index)`; the type makes multi-interval assembly unrepresentable rather than merely checked.
  - A non-`transcript` assertion carries `proposal_id`, `shown_text_digest`, `config_digest`, and a `ConfirmationDecision(decision, timestamp)`. `NoteProposal` is structurally distinct from `NoteAssertion`, so an unconfirmed proposal cannot be written by construction.
  - `GeneratedNote` additionally carries `session_id` (bound, `SESSION_ID_PATTERN`), `template_profile_id`, the confirmed cluster→role assignment, `transcript_digest`, and `config_digest`. **Define `transcript_digest` precisely**: algorithm, version tag, and exact byte domain — the decrypted canonical `TranscriptDocument.to_bytes()` output, not ciphertext — computed in one place and verified identically by `read_note` and `complete_session`.
  - Done when: types import cleanly, mypy strict passes, a round-trip test passes, and tests assert that a `NoteProposal` cannot be placed in a `GeneratedSection`, that a `transcript` assertion spanning two intervals is unrepresentable, and that a non-`transcript` assertion without a `ConfirmationDecision` is rejected at construction.
  - **Done 2026-08-04.** `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py`. All three structural rejections are pinned in `tests/test_note.py`. Decisions taken against the code, recorded so later phases inherit them rather than re-litigate:
    - `NoteAssertion.note_span` is ONE `NoteSpan`, and `SourceCoords` is a 3-field `NamedTuple` (not a model), so two intervals fail validation at construction — no list of spans, no list of coordinate triples.
    - Construction requires the confirmation record's PRESENCE, its `confirmed` value, and a matching `proposal_id`, but deliberately does NOT recompute `shown_text_digest` against the text: that verification is `write_note`'s (Task 6.2), and making it unrepresentable here would leave 6.2's defence-in-depth check untestable.
    - `role_unconfirmed` is likewise left to Check 3, not enforced by the type: a clinician-owned section populated with no confirmed role must stay REACHABLE so 5.3 can fire on it.
    - `transcript_digest` = `sha256-v1:<hex>` over the decrypted canonical `TranscriptDocument.to_bytes()`. `digest_bytes()` is the single primitive; `complete_session` digests the transcript plaintext it already holds, so nothing re-serialises.
    - `NoteWarning` carries `note_warning_code` + `severity` validated against `NOTE_WARNING_SEVERITY`, so a check cannot re-grade `mapping_drop` to error later. Codes registered now are Check 3's five; Tasks 5.1/5.2/5.4 EXTEND the registry.

A behaviour whose failure class the fixture cannot express (no laterality / number / negation / name token) raises `NoteProviderError` instead of silently degrading into a different, quietly-passing class — that would let a Phase 5 cell go green on the wrong evidence.

The Critical-Constraint role gate is enforced NOW (clinician-owned sections take only confirmed-clinician, non-interrogative utterances); 2.2 supplies the preselection heuristic behind `clinician_speaker`, not the gate.

Without those, a lone `GeneratedSection`, `NoteAssertion`, `NoteWarning` or `ConfirmationDecision` repr — and their JSON, which no class-name signature could catch — passed the filter. `NoteUtterance` needs nothing new: it holds `TranscriptWord`s under the already-registered `transcript_words`.

If `note.enc` exists: fsync + verify-decrypt + parse + session-binding + `transcript_digest` match, all before `delete_session_key`. **Fails closed, symmetric with the transcript** — any failure retains the key and Complete does not proceed. Retaining custody is precisely what keeps regeneration possible; deleting the note and completing would delete the key too, making the transcript unreadable and regeneration impossible.

### Phase 2 — Speaker roles and diarization measurement
- [x] 🟩 2.1: Per-segment cepstral mean normalisation in `_segment_embedding` (`transcription.py:507-543`), to remove the gain nuisance that makes one loud and one quiet speaker separable as two clusters.
  - Done when: existing diarization tests pass and a new test shows a gain-shifted copy of a segment clusters with its original.
  - **Done 2026-08-04.** One line of behaviour (`log_mel = log_mel - log_mel.mean()`) plus the reasoning that justifies it. A gain `g` scales every power by `g²`, so it adds the SAME constant `2·ln(g)` to all 24 log-mel bands; subtracting the segment's mean across bands removes that constant exactly. Equivalent to per-frame removal followed by the time average (the means commute), so it is applied to the already-averaged vector.
  - **Only the mel block is normalised.** The centroid is a power RATIO and already gain-invariant — the pre-fix run measured its gain sensitivity at `1.7e-4` against `4.61` per mel band. Folding it into the mean would mix two units and reintroduce a level dependence.
  - **Pure tones cannot test this and the first attempt at the fixture was wrong.** A tone's band energies are knife-edge functions of where it falls in the triangular filterbank, so a frequency change swamps any level change: a 220 Hz/2600 Hz fixture passed BEFORE the fix and proved nothing. The suite now uses `comb_pcm` (test helper) — a harmonic comb with `1/k**rolloff` decay, which puts real energy in every mel band so a level change moves every band equally, while `rolloff` varies spectral tilt the way two speakers do. `fundamental` must divide `SAMPLE_RATE` so one period tiles (exact and fast).
  - **All three new tests were run against the pre-fix code and all three failed**, with the predicted signature: a uniform `4.6086…4.6100` offset in every mel band, and the 2×2 loud/quiet × two-voice fixture clustering by LEVEL (`[s1, s2, s2, s1]`) instead of by voice. Tests: `test_gain_shift_leaves_the_embedding_unchanged` (feature-level, pins the mechanism), `test_a_gain_shifted_copy_clusters_with_its_original` (the Done-when), `test_two_voices_stay_separable_across_a_level_difference` (guards the downside — normalising away loudness must not normalise away spectral shape).
  - Both call sites are covered without change: `_segment_embedding` is the single feature function used by `label_speakers` and by the inline windowed path in `transcribe_session:901`, so the paired degenerate-case policy at `:907` is untouched.
- [x] 🟩 2.2: `speaker_role()` — **preselect** which cluster is the clinician from talk-time share, question-asking rate, and first-speaker order. The result is a default for the mandatory confirmation in Task 7.5, never an authority. The clinician-owned sections (`assessment`, `diagnosis`, `advice_home_exercise`, `management_plan`) populate only from confirmed-clinician utterances; unresolved or merged clustering leaves them blank with candidate quotations surfaced instead.
  - A clinician's *question* is not a diagnosis: cue matching into clinician-owned sections must exclude interrogative forms.
  - Done when: role preselection is a pure function with fixture tests; a test asserts clinician-owned sections stay empty when the role is unconfirmed; and a test asserts an interrogative clinician utterance does not populate `diagnosis`.
  - **Done 2026-08-04.** `note.speaker_role()` — pure, 13 fixture tests. **Two of the three Done-when clauses were already met by Phase 1** and are pinned by tests that predate this task: `test_clinician_owned_sections_stay_blank_without_a_confirmed_role` and `test_a_clinician_question_is_not_a_diagnosis` (with `test_clinician_imperative_advice_reaches_the_note` guarding the round-1 MED-001 direction). This task added the preselector and the end-to-end link between them.
  - **It lives in `note.py`, not `transcription.py`, and that is forced rather than chosen.** The question signal needs `is_interrogative`, and `note.py` already imports `transcription`; putting the function the other way round would be an import cycle. It sits directly above `ExtractiveNoteProvider`, next to the routing rule it feeds.
  - **What stops a preselection becoming an authority is the RETURN TYPE.** `speaker_role()` returns `SpeakerRolePreselection`, not the `str` that `clinician_speaker` accepts, so reaching the label costs an explicit `.preselected_clinician_speaker` — visible at the call site and not skippable under `mypy strict`. Nothing in the pipeline calls it yet; Task 7.5 owns the control that converts it into a confirmed role.
  - **Weights, and their honest status:** question rate 0.60, first speaker 0.25, talk-time share 0.15. They are authored against consultation structure, NOT measured — **Task 2.3 is what confirms or reverses them**. The talk-time term is the only one whose DIRECTION is an assumption (history-taking means the patient talks more; explanation and exercise instruction mean the clinician does), which is why it is weighted to break near-ties rather than to decide. `test_question_rate_outranks_both_talk_time_and_speaking_first` pins that ordering: a speaker holding >80% of talk time AND the first-speaker bonus still loses to a consistent question-asker.
  - **The question rate is additively smoothed** (`questions / (utterances + 1)`): on the raw rate a speaker with one interrogative sentence scores 1.0 and outranks a clinician who asked six questions in nine turns. `test_a_lone_question_does_not_outrank_a_sustained_asker` builds exactly that case — and uses a third speaker label, which also shows the function does not assume exactly two clusters.
  - **No preselection at all** for an empty transcript, for a MERGED single-label clustering (no second cluster to choose against — it must not fall back to "the only speaker"), or for a tie between the top two. `test_an_unresolved_preselection_leaves_clinician_owned_sections_blank` carries that through to the note: merged clustering → `None` → every clinician-owned section empty.
  - **The tie rule uses a `1e-9` float-noise epsilon, not a confidence threshold.** Genuinely tied clusters can miss exact equality by an ulp once weights are summed. There is deliberately no "too close to call" threshold above it: what that should be is a question about measured accuracy, i.e. Task 2.3's. `margin` is returned so Task 7.5 can present a weak preselection as weak; this function applies no threshold to it.
  - **Segments that transcribed to no text are excluded from BOTH signals**, so the two rates are measured over one population — counting a wordless turn as a turn would silently depress that speaker's question rate.
  - **`SpeakerEvidence` carries counts and seconds only, never transcript text**, because a role preselection is exactly what a UI renders and a diagnostic logs (Critical Constraint: no clinical content in logs). `test_carries_no_transcript_text` asserts it through `repr()`. Candidate quotations for Task 7.5 are deliberately NOT here — that screen holds the transcript and can quote from it directly.
- [ ] 🟥 2.3: Measure role and 2-way cluster accuracy on **several labelled human recordings** (not one), before and after 2.1, including at least one three-speaker recording. Record the numbers in this plan.
  - Done when: the measurements are recorded and D-S1 can be decided on evidence rather than a single sample.
- [ ] 🟥 D-S1: Estimated-k speaker counting — adopt or defer.  `[decision]`
  - Options: (a) implement k-selection over k ∈ {1..4}; (b) keep k=2 and defer estimated-k to the ONNX embedding upgrade
  - Decide after: Task 2.3's measured 2-way accuracy
  - Blocks: nothing in this plan — the extractive provider and the checker work under either outcome
  - If (a): cap k at n−1 (`_kmeans_two:553` raises `ValueError` otherwise), handle silhouette being undefined at k=1 with an explicit threshold rule, guard NaN from duplicate points (`argmax` selects NaN as maximum; `test_transcription.py:414-419` already feeds duplicates), update **all four** guard sites, and replace `:585-586`'s binary labelling with first-appearance ordering.

- [x] 🟩 4.0: **Atomic-assertion authoring in the config schema.** Autofill expansions and prefill seeds are authored as explicit LISTS of atomic assertions in the config file — never as prose that gets split at runtime. Pinned at model level and END-TO-END through `load_note_config` (the message survives into `NoteConfigInvalidError`, tagged with the filename to edit). Small in-scope schema addition, same class as the existing trigger check: a `region_keywords` entry with no content tokens "could never fire" and is now rejected at construction (logged as an auto-disposed LOW fold-in — it guards the same authoring surface Task 4.0 owns). Suites 886 → 889. **Amended by Round 15 PR-HIGH-001 (2026-08-10, practitioner-disposed fix):** outer list shape alone was NOT proof of atomicity — entry-level guards now also enforce it: a lexical compound-entry check (`[.!?;]` + whitespace + more text; trailing terminators and decimals pinned accepted) with an explicit per-entry `single_claim` override (`SingleClaimEntry` for expansions, a flag on seeds — the author states it, no parser decides), and normalised duplicate rejection (expansion entries; `(section, seed_text)` pairs). What is enforced is exactly that and no more — conjunction-joined compounds and a falsely-applied override remain authoring-time human error outside any mechanical check, stated in the code. Suites 913 → 931. **Amended again by Round 16 PR-MED-001 (2026-08-11, practitioner-disposed Option B): the round-15 DETECTOR description above is SUPERSEDED** — a no-space semicolon join leaked through it (third separator form of the family), so the deny-list was replaced by the `_is_atomic_shape` ALLOW-LIST: an entry is accepted only when every character matches the conservative single-claim shape (documented exhaustively in `note_config.py`), and any unforeseen separator fails closed by construction. Override and duplicate semantics unchanged. Enforced surface and residue restated in code: the surviving compounds are comma-splice/conjunction joins inside the allowed alphabet, plus override misuse. Suites 931 → 949. **Amended a third time by Round 17 PR-MED-001 (2026-08-11, fix-induced, auto-routed):** the reframe's chain-continuation allowance was itself a leak (`"q.i.d. ice applied."` — continuation-vs-new-claim is semantic), closed by strict narrowing: letter-dot chains are TERMINAL-only, any text after the chain-final dot refuses into the override. The residue is now a DERIVED list, not prose (three falsifications in three rounds): comma/conjunction joins, hyphen-between-alphanumerics joins, digit-boundary joins — each mechanically inseparable from legitimate `mid-back`/`X-ray`/`1.5mg` wording — plus override misuse; accepted by design with Phase 7 confirmation and Phase 5 checking as compensating controls. Suites 949 → 952. **Amended a FOURTH time by Round 18 PR-MED-001 (2026-08-11, docs-only):** even the round-17 "derived" list was an enumeration, and it omitted the unrestricted in-claim punctuation category (`/`, `+`, `&`, parentheses, quotes, operators — all representable joins). The residue statement is now COMPLETE-BY-CONSTRUCTION: defined by REFERENCE to `_ALLOWED_IN_CLAIM_PUNCT` and the shape's permissive branches (the set is the source of truth, prose gives representative examples only), with the doc↔code sync pinned by two mechanical tests (every set member accepted mid-claim in both models; the by-reference markers asserted in both modules' source). Code unchanged — every member is accepted by design because legitimate clinical wording needs it. Suites 952 → 954.

Never an assertion, never one proposal for a multi-claim expansion.

Decisions recorded in the module docstring, each deliberate: (1) tokenisation is `content_tokens` applied PER WORD — the single source, so fillers inside a spoken trigger don't defeat the match; (2) matching is contiguous WITHIN ONE SEGMENT — a trigger straddling a VAD boundary fails toward silence, never toward a wrong candidate; (3) a rule fires on its FIRST occurrence and carries that start time — repeats add no evidence, only confirmation fatigue; (4) both emitters CANONICALISE the incoming config (`_canonical_config`, the round-12 boundary lesson) so the stamped `config_digest` derives from re-validated field data — forged/`model_construct` configs die typed, pinned. That proves DIFFERENCE, not CLASS — a cell can return a different result for the wrong reason, exercise the wrong checker or none, and still look like valid evidence that the named class was generated.

Either project `speaker` into the generic fingerprint or register such behaviours with their own oracle.
  - **A correction to the Phase-1 executor's reasoning that matters for how postconditions are written.** Phase 1 argued that an `invented_*` behaviour whose fixed text duplicates transcript content still produces its class "because it lands in a clinician-owned section". The peer showed that is not what preserves the class: what actually does is COORDINATE MISMATCH — the fixed multiword assertion cites only the first transcript word, so exact reconstruction fails. A hand-authored one-word `TranscriptWord` holding the whole phrase would defeat that reasoning entirely. **Do not rely on section ownership as the proof; write a behaviour-specific postcondition asserting the class directly.** That is the sound form for all of them.
  - **This class has now recurred in rounds 1, 4, 5 and 6** (laterality case-sensitivity → zero-doses and `Wilson` → punctuation/case cosmetics → wrong-class/invalid output). Every previous fix closed the named instances and left the class open. A per-behaviour postcondition asserted by an independent oracle is the shape that ends it; a generic difference check is the shape that has not.

(2) `autofill_trigger_absent` re-resolves each autofill assertion's rule through its content-derived proposal id (`note_fill._proposal_id` recomputed over rules × entries) and re-verifies the trigger with `note_fill._first_phrase_match` — the SINGLE matcher, per the Phase 4 handoff pointer, so a legitimately-fired rule can never be flagged by divergent semantics; an unresolvable id (forged, or authored under another config digest) fails CLOSED into the same code. (3) `role_unconfirmed` fires per populated clinician-owned section when the role is unresolved (provenance-agnostic — the constraint is categorical, even explicit confirmation does not substitute for the session role), AND per-assertion when a clinician-owned section holds a transcript assertion whose SOURCE SEGMENT speaker — derived from coordinates, never trusted from the assertion's display `speaker` field — is not the confirmed clinician: that is the spoken-injection defence (the `obeys_injection` matrix cell errors on exactly this), and a lying speaker field cannot defeat it (pinned). (4) `mapping_drop` delegates to `note_config.mapping_drop_warnings` — single source. Recorded NOT-checked residue: prefill assertions carry no trigger claim to re-verify; a wrong speaker label OUTSIDE clinician-owned sections is Task 7.6's review surface; `shown_text_digest`-vs-text is `write_note`'s (Task 6.2), deliberately not duplicated.

Task 7.6 carries the rest presentationally.

Decisions recorded: coverage counts coordinate CARRIAGE, not faithfulness (a mutated-but-citing assertion is Check 1's error, not a second omission); `clinician_speaker=None` ⇒ the scoping predicate does not exist ⇒ the check emits nothing (honest, pinned); severity is review because a heuristic must not block Complete. The stated limit is in code verbatim: high-risk-token heuristic, not a materiality classifier; `clinically_material_span_ids` lives ONLY in fixtures (`material_segments` in the matrix) with no runtime producer. The three shared lexicons are documented first-cut, non-evidence, closed (fail toward silence).

After hardening rounds 19–24 (in-session loop + the full four-round cross-family peer pass with fix legs, converged by practitioner decision at round 24): pytest **1123**, matrix 67 instances (incl. 4 clean compositions with matched-pair witnesses, full declared oracles on every non-raising cell, and two corruption-bar meta-tests), check suite 72.

### Phase 6 — Pipeline and controller plumbing
- [x] 🟩 6.1: The two-stage pipeline in `note.py`, matching Flow 1's ordering exactly. `compose_draft()` takes a resolved provider plus the config-resolved rules and returns the base note **plus proposals**, running no checks. `finalise_note()` takes the confirmed proposals **with their `ConfirmationDecision` records**, composes assertions, then runs **every** check and assembles `GeneratedNote`. Checks never run before confirmation, and confirmation evidence rides the artifact rather than the call.
  - Done when: `finalise_note()` refuses a note containing an unconfirmed proposal; refuses a non-`transcript` assertion whose `shown_text_digest` does not match the text actually confirmed; and confirmed clinician-authored assertions pass through Checks 2 and 3.
  - **Done (2026-08-11, executor `stage-6`).** `compose_draft(document, config, provider, template_profile_id=None, *, clinician_speaker=None, prefill_id=None) -> NoteDraft` and `finalise_note(draft, resolutions, document, config, *, created_at=None) -> GeneratedNote` in `note.py`, plus three new types: `NoteDraft` (the UNCHECKED stage-one hand-off — in-memory only, never persisted; inherits the structural guarantees from its member types, so it cannot smuggle unconfirmed content), `ProposalResolution` (the Flow 1 confirmation-evidence bundle exactly as the plan words it: the `shown_text_digest` of what the review surface actually RENDERED — deliberately not copied from the proposal — plus the `ConfirmationDecision`), and typed errors `NotePipelineError`/`ProposalEvidenceError`. Decisions recorded in code, each pinned: (1) compose runs NO checks and finalise calls `check_note` exactly once — pinned by monkeypatch call-count, not prose. (2) An UNRESOLVED proposal is not an exception: it flows into `check_note(pending_proposals=...)` and comes back as an `unconfirmed_proposal` ERROR riding the artifact — the property-4 ACTION-STATE refusal (`blocking_warnings()` non-empty, `write_note` refuses), matching the Phase 5 handoff's stated contract for that parameter. (3) A resolution — confirmed OR declined, widened decision-agnostic in round 25 LOW-001 — whose `shown_text_digest` is not the digest of the proposal's exact insertable text raises `ProposalEvidenceError` (a mis-rendered decline must not silently resolve a proposal the clinician never saw); so do a resolution naming a proposal the draft never emitted and two resolutions for one proposal. A declined proposal composes nothing and IS resolved (the type also pins: a declined decision cannot construct an assertion). (4) A stale document/config dies in `check_note`'s digest gate — deliberately not re-verified in finalise (one gate, one owner). (5) Section merge is deterministic: canonical order, provider assertions first, confirmed proposals after in draft order; composed assertions reuse the proposal id as assertion id (content-derived, collision-free with provider ids). (6) `compose_draft` constructs its request ONLY through `note_config.build_note_request` (call-time import; the AST reference-confinement guard is untouched — no new runtime `NoteRequest` reference exists, exact node counts unchanged) and its `clinician_speaker` must be the CONFIRMED role per the Task 7.5 guard. Both new models joined the Task 1.4 tripwire coverage list. Confirmed clinician-authored assertions pass Checks 2 AND 3: the fully-confirmed pipeline note's only warnings are the unsuppressible `clinician_asserted` reviews (pinned). Tests in NEW `desktop/tests/test_note_pipeline.py`.
- [x] 🟩 6.2: `write_note`/`read_note` mirroring `write_transcript`/`read_transcript`, over `atomic_write_bytes`, no AAD. Unlink a stale `note.enc` before `write_transcript` writes, so re-transcription cannot leave a note describing a superseded transcript.
  - **`write_note` enforces the invariants itself**, as defense in depth rather than trusting the UI: it refuses on any unresolved `error`, and it verifies every non-`transcript` assertion against its `ConfirmationDecision` and `shown_text_digest`, refusing an unbacked one.
  - `read_note` verifies `session_id` binding and `transcript_digest` using the single definition from Task 1.1 — the same code path `complete_session` uses.
  - **Done (2026-08-11, executor `stage-6`).** `write_note(session_dir, crypto, note, config)` / `read_note(session_dir, crypto)` in `session_store.py`, both over `atomic_write_bytes`, no AAD (the `write_transcript` docstring's recorded reason: `complete_session` verifies with a plain decrypt). The verification CORE is now the shared `_verified_note` (decrypt → parse → session binding → transcript-digest match): `_verify_note_for_completion` was refactored onto it and `read_note` calls it — the "same code path" Done-when is pinned STRUCTURALLY by a monkeypatch spy counting both callers, not by prose. `write_note`'s unbacked-assertion enforcement follows the no-content-escapes lesson — it confines the CLASS, not a field list: the note is first CANONICALISED (`_canonical_note`, the round-12 `_canonical_config` boundary shape: base-class serializer over field data + full re-validation), so every construction invariant (confirmation presence, confirmed-not-declined, proposal-id match) is re-established at once and a `model_construct`/subclass forgery dies typed (pinned); then the two relations construction deliberately does NOT verify are checked explicitly — `shown_text_digest` must be the digest of the assertion's OWN text, and each assertion's `config_digest` must equal the note's (evidence under a different config backs a different proposal; real coverage for prefill, whose staleness Check 3 cannot re-verify). Then: refuse on `blocking_warnings()` non-empty (the property-4 state), re-verify `config_digest` against the presented canonicalised config, `session_id` against `_resolve_session_identity`, and `transcript_digest` against the decrypted ON-DISK transcript. Every refusal is typed `NoteWriteRefusedError` and leaves the disk unchanged. The stale-note unlink lives INSIDE `write_transcript` (covers both transcription and recovery paths), unlink-FIRST and fail-closed: an unremovable stale note aborts the transcript write (`StoreWriteError`), because the hazard is exactly the stale (note, transcript) pairing; a crash between unlink and write loses only an already-superseded note. Tests: `TestNoteArtifactIO` (15) in `test_session_store.py` + 2 unlink tests in `test_transcription.py`.
- [x] 🟩 6.3: **A controller-owned `generating` lease**, not UI guards alone. Prefer a controller operation that performs scoped artifact access over exposing raw directory/crypto accessors. While the lease is held, block `start()`, `complete()`, `discard()`, session retirement, and recovered-key destruction. Add the lease to `SessionControllerLike` and `FakeController`.
  - The UI-guard-only design was insufficient: `start()` (`session.py:285-290`) calls `_retire_locked` (`:554`) on a **queued** session, dropping the in-memory handle a generation worker depends on — and `SessionController` documents any-thread safety, so a UI button state cannot be the guard.
  - **The lease is a TOKEN spanning the whole operation, not the worker.** Acquire before the `TaskThread` starts; release only after the GUI-thread `write_note` succeeds or failure cleanup completes. A worker-scoped lease released on callable return leaves the custody-critical gap exactly where `write_note` runs.
  - **Recovered sessions bypass `SessionController` entirely** — `main_window.py:194-220` calls store primitives directly — so controller guards alone cannot protect them. Route live AND recovered Complete / Discard / key-destruction through one lease-aware custody coordinator.
  - Done when: tests cover `start()`-, Complete-, Discard-, and `_destroy_recovered_crypto`-during-generation on both live and recovered paths, plus a barrier test firing exactly after worker return and before `write_note`.
  - **Done (2026-08-11, executor `stage-6`).** `GenerationLease` (identity-compared opaque token, no state to forge) + `GenerationInProgressError` in `session.py`; `SessionController.begin_generation()` / `end_generation(lease)` / `generating`. The lease is deliberately COARSE — one per controller, state-agnostic (a recovered generation runs with no live session at all), at most one in flight; over-blocking fails toward safety. `end_generation` is idempotent for the released token (cleanup may run twice) but REFUSES a token that is not the held one. While held, `_refuse_while_generating` (under the controller lock) blocks `start()` (checked at entry, before any side effect), `complete()`, `discard()`, and — defense in depth, the guard lives at the mechanism not only today's callers — `_retire_locked` itself. The RECOVERED path routes through three new coordinator operations that perform the scoped custody action inside the same lease check and lock: `complete_recovered(directory, crypto)` (Flow 2 ordering via `complete_session`), `discard_recovered(directory, crypto)`, `destroy_recovered_crypto(crypto)`; `main_window.py`'s recovered callbacks and `_destroy_recovered_crypto` now call ONLY these (raw `complete_session`/`discard_session` imports removed from the window). On a refused in-memory-key destruction the window RETAINS the reference so the worker's key survives and the release path re-runs the cleanup — Phase 7's busy guards own keeping view swaps unreachable during generation (recorded residue, not smoothed). All five ops added to `SessionControllerLike` and `FakeController` (fake has a configurable `generation_error`). Two DELIBERATE non-extensions, recorded in the coordinator's comment block (wording corrected in round 25 LOW-003): the 24 h expiry sweep is NOT lease-blocked — the shipped wiring already keeps it away from every session a generation could target (`app.sweep_protected_ids` covers the controller's non-terminal session incl. QUEUED, round 42 MED-001, plus the recovery checkouts, PR round 18), and if that wiring ever changed the race still fails CLOSED (key destroyed → `write_note`/Complete refuse); and `recovery.py`'s list-discard needs no lease (a checked-out generating session is excluded from the listing and list actions are disabled while any checkout exists). Done-when tests: `TestGenerationLeaseTokens` + `TestGenerationLeaseRecoveredPath` + `TestGenerationLeaseLivePath` in `test_session_machine.py` — start/Complete/Discard-during-generation on the live queued session (handle + custody proven untouched by the refusals), Complete/Discard/key-destruction-during-generation on the recovered path, the BARRIER test (worker returned, `write_note` not yet run → still refused; released only by explicit `end_generation`), and the worker-failure variant (lease held until cleanup releases) — plus 4 `main_window` routing tests in `test_ui_screens.py` including `_destroy_recovered_crypto`-during-generation retaining the reference.

  - **Done (2026-08-11, executor `stage-6`) — HONESTLY SCOPED: fixture transcripts, not a real recording.** `TestExtractivePipelineEndToEnd` in `test_note_pipeline.py` runs `ExtractiveNoteProvider` through the REAL new pipeline end to end — `write_transcript` → `compose_draft` → per-proposal confirmation → `finalise_note` → `write_note` → `read_note` → `complete_session` — over a four-utterance fixture consultation, asserting the note's exact verbatim contents and that the artifact clears the Complete ordering with the key destroyed. A genuinely real transcript needs the practitioner and the app, so the BINDING usability judgment is Task 9.1's shipping gate; nothing was invented to simulate it. ### Phase 8 — Documentation
- [x] 🟩 8.1: Threat-model re-review covering this phase's surfaces — config as a note-content input, clinician-asserted content in a clinical record, the extended in-memory transcript lifetime across the review window, and the **ratified copyable-note change**. Record the checker's honest limit: in 3A, `transcript` spans are exact by coordinate reconstruction — recombination is rejected structurally, not detected — but clinician-authored spans are verified only against transcript *contradiction*, so a confirmed assertion the transcript is merely silent about is not detectable by any check. That residual is carried by per-assertion clinician confirmation and by the clinician's own review at signing (`PLAN.md:14`), and must be stated rather than implied.
